# B2: Material map of the admittance_control documentation for the thesis

*Built 2026-10-20 from every Markdown/text document of `ros2_ws/src/admittance_control` (README, `notes/`, `thesis/THESIS_PLAN.md`, `thesis/cago_notes_about_current_thesis.md`, the small READMEs, the literature reports in `thesis/paper_pool`) against the draft chapters of 2026-10-16 (`thesis/manuscript/chapters/ch3_methodology_cell.tex`, `ch5_experimental_setup.tex`, `ch6_results.tex`; ch1, ch2, ch7 and the appendices for the section map). Read-only review: nothing in the repository was changed.*

@@DOC notes/thesis_notes.md (TN)
- **Covers:** the running thesis log ("what happened / evidence / what we learned"), newest first, from 2026-09-28 to 2026-10-16: the cable wind-up false contact and the aim buzz (10-16); the finger-pointing aim and multi-view on curved parts (10-15); true-shape neighbours, the curved bench without multi-view, Cartesian vs APS transits, pen contacts as a probe, planner margin (10-14); the first curved bench, roll per tack, fitting printed parts to primitives (10-09); tracker-is-not-a-registration, the C++ collision model, live FP tracking split (10-06); the no-touch-sensing / FP-tracking decisions, the ~2.5 mm headline, multi-view after the depth fix, the depth z² law and the Tare (10-02); earlier lessons (09-29 kinematics, straddle, holder model, unmodelled plate, motion safety; 10-01 registration-to-marking time, multi-view synthetic findings).
- **Status:** current; it is the authoritative dated record of numbers (the README §14 and the draft's ch6 table are built from it). Older entries are not edited when superseded; later entries supersede them (e.g. 10-02 "next step: anchor the height to the table" is still open in TD; 10-06 C++ model and 10-14 margin cap supersede the "planning took minutes" state).
- **Decisions held (dated):** no touch sensing in the method, touches only for calibration and independent measurement (10-02 evening); FP tracking instead of ICP tracking, Kalman fusion dropped "for now" (10-02 evening); register on the desktop, track on the laptop (10-06); ICP at `save_object` after FP tracking (10-06 evening); C++ collision model + OMPL APS (10-06 evening); resting-contact pull OFF by default, gap measured and warned instead (10-02); absolute observability criterion 0.5 mm std replacing the 5 % relative one (10-02); do NOT promote self-calibration from the 10-02 runs (10-02 15:00); roll per tack on curved seams (10-09 evening); pipes as 6 rotated boxes / sector boxes / box chains in the collision model (10-09 evening); departure points 100 mm out + Cartesian line first, APS as fallback (10-14 afternoon); true-shape neighbours in multi-view (10-14 night); acceptance rules "apply a correction within the limits" (10-15); spin settled by physics for cut pipes (10-15); wrist_3 window ±180° about home; overshoot ends at the table (10-16); aim `rate_hz` 50 → 125 and the C++ 500 Hz relay (10-16); "measure accuracy with dots, demonstrate with strokes" (10-14 evening).
- **Feeds:** 3.1, 3.3 (all), 3.4.2, 3.5.2-3.5.4, 3.6 (all), 3.7.1, 3.7.4, 3.9 (all), 3.10.2, 5.4, 5.5, 6.1-6.9, 6.11, 7.
@@END
@S 3.1 | Decision 2026-10-02 (evening): no touch sensing in the method; "a scenario where the parts cannot be touched must remain valid"; touches stay in two roles only: calibration (pen TCP, table plane, the Tare's ground-truth distance) and independent measurement of the error (`touch_probe.py`); "never a step that corrects a tack" — TN 2026-10-02 (evening)
@S 3.1 | Evidence given for the decision: every millimetre from 8 mm down to 2.5 mm came from vision and calibration (ICP normal gate, depth Tare, extrinsic tilt, multi-view refinement), not from contact — TN 2026-10-02 (evening)
@S 3.1 | The full chain as run for the ~2.5 mm headline: calibrated kinematics → Tare-corrected depth → refined extrinsic tilt → registration → `refine_pose` (4 close views, online self-calibration iterated, prior corrected by R_scan·d, gap measured) → `welding_points` → reachability → marking — TN 2026-10-02 (15:00)
@S 3.1 | The force-gated pen is also a probe: its contacts measure the registration error per joint with no extra hardware; "That is evaluation only; the cell stays vision-only" — TN 2026-10-14
@S 3.3 | Order of calibration matters: robot kinematics, then the pen TCP and touched plane, then camera depth (Tare), then the extrinsic (tilt, then translation); "A step done on top of an uncorrected earlier one learns that earlier error" — TN 2026-10-02 depth entry, lesson 5
@S 3.3 | "A touch reference is what exposed it": the registration agreed with its own camera to well under a millimetre (`check_registration`: base +0.3 mm, ear −0.1 mm) while being ~7 mm off in the robot's frame (pen on base top +6.0/+7.7 mm, ear faces +0.1/+3.8 mm); "Vision checking vision can't see a shared bias" — TN 2026-10-02 depth entry
@S 3.3.1 | Kinematic model mismatch (2026-09-29): TF and the planner used nominal UR5e kinematics, the robot its factory calibration; 0.55° at the camera and 2.4–4.2 mm at the pen across poses; lesson: every frame consumer must use the same kinematic model; shared code now carries the model and reports record which one they used — TN Earlier/2026-09-29
@S 3.3.1 | Table plane re-measured with the pen on 2026-10-02: 7 touches, new centre (−0.143, 0.426), z −96.15 mm; the old plane was from 28 Sep and by 29 Sep the pen read 1–3 mm off it — TN 2026-10-02 depth, Fix attempt 2
@S 3.3.1 | The table is tilted 1.57°; "straight down" in world terms was 1.69° off square-to-table; the table's own tilt at the checked spot came out as 0.44° (not a camera error) — TN 2026-10-02 depth, Fix attempt 2
@S 3.3.2 | Tilt separation: the same table spot viewed at four wrist yaws (−130, −53, 46, 140°; 270° spread; 0.46 m range); camera-fixed part 0.49°, world-fixed (table vs pen plane) 0.44°, residual 0.05°; a single-yaw check would have mixed the two — TN 2026-10-02 depth
@S 3.3.2 | `refine_extrinsic_from_table.py` gave a correction of 0.488° about the camera's x axis (fit residual 0.07°, conditioning 1.00), moving points 2.6 mm at 300 mm range; written as `T_tcp_to_cam_refined.npy` and promoted — TN 2026-10-02 depth
@S 3.3.2 | Re-check after promotion (wrist at −136, −46, 44, 134°): every view's tilt points the same way, 137–148°, 0.36–0.51°, mean 0.44° toward 143° (= the table's tilt); the part that turns with the wrist (camera) ≈ 0.07°, down from 0.49°; height +1.4 to +2.5 mm, a constant ≈ +2.0 mm at 0.46 m (camera places surfaces ~2 mm high) — TN 2026-10-02 depth
@S 3.3.3 | Symptom: after the kinematics, ICP and holder fixes every pen mark met the surface early, 8.3–8.8 mm along the pen on all four tacks, both sides of the T; marks 3–7 mm off — TN 2026-10-02 depth, What happened
@S 3.3.3 | Diagnosis from the multi-view capture (4 views at 0.4 m): base top vs registration = pen touches +5.9/+7.8 mm; 4 close views (range 364–477 mm) +3.9/+4.1/+4.3/+4.3 mm; scan-home view (~609 mm) 0 by definition; so ~6.9 mm low at scan home, ~2.8 mm low in close views; predicted ratio linear-in-range 1.8, range² 2.6, measured 2.5 — TN 2026-10-02 depth, Diagnosis
@S 3.3.3 | The range² law is the stereo signature: a disparity offset δ gives z²·δ/(f·B); fit error = a + k·z² with k ≈ −17 mm/m² (depth reported too long), predicts the middle point to 0.2 mm (a line misses by 0.7 mm); implied disparity offset ≈ 0.6 px; k = −17 mm/m² ≈ 6.6 mm at 0.62 m and 2.7 mm at 0.4 m — TN 2026-10-02 depth, Confirmation
@S 3.3.3 | Fix attempt 1 (on-chip calibration + Tare, distance entered by hand) overshot: error flipped sign and became linear (+11.1 mm per metre, middle point on the line to 0.01 mm) = a depth SCALE error ≈ 1.1 %, reading too short; hypothesis (not confirmed): the hand ground truth was measured from the wrong origin — the D435/D435i depth origin sits 4.2 mm behind the front glass; a few mm wrong at 0.3–0.6 m = 0.7–1.4 % scale — TN 2026-10-02 depth, attempt 1
@S 3.3.3 | Fix attempt 2: Tare ground truth computed in the robot's frame by `scripts/tare_distance.py` (camera pose from `/joint_states` through the calibrated kinematics and the extrinsic file; depth along the optical axis to the pen plane); camera square to the TABLE (tilted 1.57°), axis on the touched patch, ~0.5 m away — TN 2026-10-02 depth, attempt 2
@S 3.3.3 | Lesson: validate depth at three or more ranges spanning the working volume (0.3–0.65 m); "The vendor's Tare is only as good as its ground-truth distance"; deriving it from the robot's frame makes the camera consistent with the frame the robot acts in — TN 2026-10-02 depth, lessons 1-2
@S 3.3.3 | Side effect: the self-calibration history logged a wrong extrinsic offset (−2.3, −2.0, 1.1) mm (the depth bias mis-attributed); the history is keyed on the extrinsic FILE but recalibrating depth also invalidates earlier runs; "The key should include the depth calibration state" — TN 2026-10-02 depth, Side effects
@S 3.4.2 | Aim mode (added 2026-10-15): before capture the operator points and the wrist camera turns until the fingertip is centred; image ~0.1 s old when the gesture is recognised; rotation only about the optical centre makes the base-frame ray to a still fingertip depth-independent, so the old image's ray turned with the camera pose at its stamp (joint history) gives the exact error in the current camera frame: "compensated exactly instead of being predicted" — TN 2026-10-15 aim
@S 3.4.2 | Lessons: "Choose the motion so that the measurement becomes easy"; stream rates, not trajectories, for a human in the loop; hand the joints back to the trajectory controller as soon as the arm is still — TN 2026-10-15 aim
@S 3.4.2 | Deadband needed hysteresis that ends INSIDE the inner radius; measured from that radius itself the camera crept towards it for ever and never let go of the controller — TN 2026-10-15 aim
@S 3.4.2 | Node bugs found against the fake cell: a logging line used both info and warn severity (rclpy raises); two aim nodes fought over the velocity controller → a node now refuses when the controller is already active — TN 2026-10-15 aim
@S 3.4.2 | Gain raised from 1.2 /s (sized for uncompensated latency) to 2.5 /s once latency was compensated — TN 2026-10-15 aim
@S 3.4.2 | Aim parameters stated: 15°/s rate cap; 64-px (≈ 4°) deadband on the 1280-px image; engagement jumps the target rate to ~8°/s — TN 2026-10-16 buzz
@S 3.4.2 | Buzz incident: wrist buzzed near the image centre; first guess "P-only not enough"; a 45 s bag (joint states 500 Hz with effort, commands 50 Hz) showed the cause was the command staircase: velocity streamed at 50 Hz to a 500 Hz controller; each step = acceleration × command period; the UR turns each step into an audible torque kick; "adding I or D would not have changed it"; between images the error is recomputed from joint states, so the camera rate is no limit on the command rate — TN 2026-10-16 buzz
@S 3.4.2 | Fix in two steps (2026-10-16): (1) `rate_hz` 50 → 125 (steps 2.5× smaller; "definitely improved" by ear, no bag); (2) C++ relay `aim_rate_relay` between the Python node and the controller, ramps linearly between the 125 Hz rates and publishes at the UR's 500 Hz, "no steps at all, for 8 ms of delay"; by-product: a `kill -9` of the aim node mid-turn used to leave its last rate on the velocity controller, now the relay ramps the arm to zero within ~0.1 s — TN 2026-10-16 buzz
@S 3.4.2 | Also proposed: "start gently near the centre" — TN 2026-10-16 buzz
@S 3.5.2 | Hardware split (2026-10-06): full-frame `register()` (SAM2 + PPF + the scorer, ~250 hypotheses) does not fit the laptop's 8 GB RTX 4060 → stays on the desktop; tracking needs only the refiner (~300 MB) → runs on the laptop in the same Docker image next to the camera; frames never cross the network; only a lost part sends one frame (plus the CAD silhouette as mask) back to the desktop to re-register — TN 2026-10-06
@S 3.5.2 | Each tracker pose is put into `base_link` with TF at its own image stamp before averaging; camera-frame poses from a moving wrist camera cannot be averaged (each lives in a different frame) — TN 2026-10-06
@S 3.5.2 | The bridge stamps detections with the registered frame's stamp instead of the reply time, so the robot pose at the seed is the one the image was taken at even when capture, click and registration took a minute; the tracker's "stale seed" rule changed: only a latched detection delivered at start-up may be stale — TN 2026-10-06
@S 3.5.2 | Why FP tracking (2026-10-02 evening): ICP runs on the CPU, each call seconds of nearest-neighbour search and Gauss–Newton, too slow for a moving part; ICP is right for a stationary object (`run_icp` seed, `refine_pose`); FP tracking registers once then refines frame to frame on the GPU at tens of Hz; Kalman fusion of FP and ICP dropped for now ("would fuse a fast tracker with a slow one that adds nothing while the part moves") — TN 2026-10-02 (evening)
@S 3.5.2 | Lesson: "split a learned pipeline by what each stage needs, not by where the software happens to run": registration rare and heavy → big GPU; tracking frequent and light → machine with the camera; both machines run the same Docker image — TN 2026-10-06
@S 3.5.2 | Incident 2026-10-09 (night): re-registration jumped to another part: when the tracker lost a part it sent the frame with a mask projected from the last pose; the desktop reclassified it and answered the plate under the part (C1 → `test_objv2_ear`, E2 → ear → base); now a re-registration naming another part is refused loudly — TN 2026-10-09 (night)
@S 3.5.2 | Open problem noted 2026-10-02: FP ran in Docker on a GPU host over Tailscale; candidates for streaming: HTTP keep-alive, WebSocket or gRPC, ROS 2 inside the container (Zenoh or discovery server across Tailscale), tracking locally on the laptop's RTX 4060 (the last was chosen 2026-10-06) — TN 2026-10-02 (evening), 2026-10-06
@S 3.5.3 | Normal-compatibility gate (2026-09-29): an 8 mm standing plate registered leaning 6.5° (its hidden back face paired with the visible face, the model straddled its own two faces); weld root 10 mm off while the fit looked fine; a 60° normal gate brought the lean to 0.3–0.8° and the root to ±0.2 mm on the same data; lesson: model-to-scene ICP needs visibility or normal gating for thin parts — TN Earlier/2026-09-29
@S 3.5.3 | ICP at save (2026-10-06 evening): with FP tracking the saved pose had become the tracker's render-and-compare estimate; result: saved parts interpenetrated 6–11 mm, `refine_pose` rejected both (overlap 3.8/4.0 mm), marks 12 mm early on one seam, no contact on the other; fix: at `save_object`, ICP on 5 fresh live clouds from the averaged tracker pose, used only when it fits and the correction stays within limits — TN 2026-10-06 (evening)
@S 3.5.3 | "A tracker answers 'where is it now', a registration answers 'where exactly is it'. Keep each for its job, and refine before freezing"; on a textureless 8 mm plate seen from ~0.6 m the FP estimate is good enough to follow a part, not to weld it — TN 2026-10-06 (evening)
@S 3.5.3 | With `tracking_source: fp` the ICP node keeps everything stationary (`run_icp`, `save_object`, `refine_pose`) and averages the tracker's frames at rest — TN 2026-10-06
@S 3.5.4 | ICP at save refused 30.8° on R2 (2026-10-09): all of it spin about the pipe's own axis, unobservable; "Symmetry must be told to the estimator. An axisymmetric part's spin is noise, so the gate measures only the tilt of the axis" — TN 2026-10-09 (night)
@S 3.5.4 | E2 (25° mitre) yielded no seam: spin registered ~36° wrong; its axis was right (25.2°), its cut 15.4° off the plate, seam gap range 26 mm; the spin that rests the cut on the plate (+36.2°) brought it to 0.4 mm; "A symmetric part's hidden feature is a blind spot": the cut is where it touches the part it stands on, out of sight; neither single- nor multi-view can measure that spin, physics can — TN 2026-10-15 multi-view
@S 3.5.4 | "The seam did not depend on the wrong number; the checks did": the intersection curve is spin-free, while the fit-up check that guarded it was not — TN 2026-10-15 multi-view
@S 3.6 | Motivation from the curved bench: a round pipe's side leaves its position along the view loose by ~8 mm, a rounded-rect tube's sides pin it to ~3 mm; "The single-view registration of a part is as good as the flat faces it shows" — TN 2026-10-14 (evening)
@S 3.6.2 | True-shape neighbours (2026-10-14 night): old neighbour model = each part's bounding box; new = each part's true shape (signed distance from its registered primitive); a box is exact for a plate and wrong for a pipe standing on a cut or wrapped round another pipe — TN 2026-10-14 (night)
@S 3.6.3 | Observability criterion changed 2026-10-02: a direction counted as "measured" only with at least 5 % of the strongest direction's information (a plate's faces carry thousands of points, so edges looked unmeasured) → absolute: measured at a std of 0.5 mm or better; with that the views alone determine the standing plate — TN 2026-10-02 MV, Resolution item 2
@S 3.6.3 | 2026-10-01 synthetic: "Determined must be judged in absolute terms": one direction of d looked weak in relative terms (4 % of the strongest) yet was measured to 0.16 mm — TN Earlier/2026-10-01
@S 3.6.3 | Limitation to report: the base's slide within its own plane still varies 1–3 mm run to run although counted as measured; it rests on grazing edge points, noisier and more correlated than the noise model assumes; does not move the weld roots but the uncertainty estimate is optimistic there — TN 2026-10-02 MV
@S 3.6.3 | A standing plate's in-plane rotation is the weak spot: a fresh registration at 14:17 put the ear 3.6° off the 14:06 one, rotated about its normal (lean 0.18° vs 0.35°); multi-view wanted 3.07°, all in-plane; only the plate's 8 mm top and end edges constrain it ("not measured: turn about its normal"); the acceptance rules rightly refused it; the old varying "fit-up gap 0.8–3.6 mm" was this tilt along the foot, not a real gap — TN 2026-10-02 MV
@S 3.6.4 | The joint fit does NOT cancel an extrinsic translation error (the part common to all views stays); estimating d and taking it out does — TN Earlier/2026-10-01
@S 3.6.4 | Why R_scan·d must also leave the prior: saved poses were registered from the scan pose and carry R_scan·d; taking d out of the views only, while holding unmeasured directions at the uncorrected saved pose, created a fake relative change (each run "changed the fit-up" ~8 mm, both parts rejected); fix: each saved object records its scan camera (`T_static_camera`) and R_scan·d is removed from the prior too — TN 2026-10-02 MV, Cause/Fix
@S 3.6.4 | d absorbs more than the extrinsic translation: view-dependent leftovers (the 1.1 mm depth trend, the 0.07° tilt residual, kinematic residuals); different view sets project them differently — TN 2026-10-02 MV
@S 3.6.4 | Persistence rule: the spread rule (1 mm) refused to write a calibration when a fourth run (other scene and view set) gave d 2.5 mm from the other three; repeat captures of one scene show repeatability not correctness; self-calibration should key runs by the registration (assembly) and require 3 distinct ones; "online" (per run: d only has to make the views agree) vs "persistent" (extrinsic file: must hold across scenes) need different evidence — TN 2026-10-02 MV
@S 3.6.4 | The vertical component is the weakly separated one: before `refine_pose` the camera placed surfaces ~2 mm high (`extrinsic_check`), the online correction lowered the parts ~7 mm, over-correcting height by ~4–5 mm; "The direction that moves every view's cloud up or down together is the one the 45°/60° views separate worst"; consequence: do NOT promote a self-calibration from these runs; next step: anchor the height to the pen-referenced table and let the online correction apply only the horizontal part of d — TN 2026-10-02 (15:00)
@S 3.6.4 | Synthetic renderer bias: Z-buffering surface samples biased every view ~1 mm toward its camera, which faked an extrinsic error; exact ray casting removed it — TN Earlier/2026-10-01
@S 3.6.5 | Acceptance rules changed (2026-10-15) to the user's principle "apply a correction within the limits": the overlap may not be worse than the saved assembly's (the better refinement had been refused, the worse saved pose kept); a pipe's spin is not judged (it does not move the seam); a pipe's bore is hollow — TN 2026-10-15 multi-view
@S 3.6.5 | Under the depth bias (before the Tare) the refinement failed safely: fit RMS 0.96 mm vs 0.35 mm synthetic; the rules rejected both parts ("fit-up changed 11.4 mm / 2.0°"), so the assembly was not corrupted; "Several views at different ranges turn a hidden bias into a measurable inconsistency" — TN 2026-10-02 depth, Side effects + lesson 4
@S 3.6.5 | The resting-contact pull (foot pulled onto the neighbour's face) overrides the data: a synthetic 3 mm gap was pulled below 1 mm unwarned; on a non-flush truth it moved the ear 1.2 mm off; so it is an option, OFF by default — TN 2026-10-02 MV, Resolution item 1
@S 3.6.5 | The feared lap-joint swap never happened; the rule meant to prevent it only cost correction range — TN Earlier/2026-10-01
@S 3.7.1 | Fitting printed parts to WeldSet primitives (2026-10-09), each fit checked both ways (CAD vertices onto the primitive; primitive vertices onto the CAD) plus the volume; five fitted, SP3 refused: its second side had been drawn as the first curve shifted 5 mm sideways, not offset along the normal; "CAD→primitive alone accepts an entry that is too long or too thick"; fitting refuses with a measured reason instead of approximating (same rule as the box registry) — TN 2026-10-09
@S 3.7.1 | SP3 redrawn as a true offset and reprinted; the fit split its cap outline at the four corners and fitted cubic splines to both sides and the midline; recovered the drawn spline: 4 control points (0, −110), (−200, 0), (200, 0), (0, 110), to 3·10⁻⁶ mm, verified at 0.05 mm; numerical lessons: solving control points and curve parameters together stalls (0.06 mm) unless started from linear fits alternated with re-projection; a "nearest dense sample" error adds the sampling gap (≈ 0.1 mm) — TN 2026-10-09 follow-up
@S 3.7.3 | With the parts registered, the seams of a pipe on a plate, a mitred pipe, a saddle, a rounded-rect tube and a curved stiffener all follow from the registered surfaces (`curved_seams.py`); pose error shows up as fit-up along the curve (e.g. 0.4–3.6 mm gap range for a 3° tilt), never as a different seam — TN 2026-10-09 follow-up
@S 3.7.4 | User requirement (2026-10-02): "an operator who places the parts too far apart must be told"; fillet root-gap limit from ISO 5817:2023 Table 1 no. 617 (already in weldgen); for 8 mm plates with a = 0.7·t: 1.06 mm (B), 1.62 mm (C), 2.68 mm (D); parts never interpenetrate (overlap rule), "a gap is information" — TN 2026-10-02 MV, Resolution
@S 3.7.4 | Near miss made visible (2026-10-09): S3 on R2 gave the right saddle (69.4° vs 70° drawn) but one end of the gap reached 10.5 mm against a 10 mm tolerance and the pair was dropped without a word; now the near miss is reported with its numbers; "A fallback that only logs makes a failure look like a result" — TN 2026-10-09 (night)
@S 3.7.4 | Unmodelled 7 mm plate (2026-09-29): an extra metal plate under the ear produced 9.7 mm early contact (7 mm / cos 45°); the registration had already reported a fit-up gap of 0.9–4.6 mm; lesson: treat unexpected fit-up gaps as a scene check — TN Earlier/2026-09-29
@S 3.7.4 | Gap measured and warned (2026-10-02): four real runs see the ear's foot 0.5–0.7 mm above the base at one end and 2.0–2.8 mm at the other, over the level C limit → WARNING; consistent across registrations (real or a systematic sensor effect); to check by hand with a feeler gauge; exact-box synthetic test: ear 3 mm off measured within 0.7 mm and warned, flush → no warning — TN 2026-10-02 MV, items 4-5
@S 3.7.4 | Fit-up warning in the headline run: the ear's foot 0.6–2.0 mm above the base against the 1.6 mm level C limit — TN 2026-10-02 (15:00)
@S 3.8 | Tack planning on the bench: C1 closed seam had 4 tacks round the 62 mm pipe — TN 2026-10-09 (evening)
@S 3.9.1 | Holder model (2026-09-29): modelled as a 42 mm capsule it cleared a square T by only 1.1 mm; some seams counted reachable only because the straddled registration had opened the corner to 96°; the envelope from the holder's CAD gives 15.5 mm; "fixing one error can unmask another" — TN Earlier/2026-09-29
@S 3.9.1 | Pipe collision boxes (2026-10-09): one box per pipe had corners 41 % of r (13 mm on C1) outside the wall where 45° tacks sit; pipes as 6 rotated boxes stand ≤ 3.3 % of r proud (1 mm on C1); mitred and saddle-cut pipes become sector boxes, each from its own lowest cut point; bands become box chains within 1 mm; the C++ planner did not change — TN 2026-10-09 (evening)
@S 3.9.2 | Roll per tack (2026-10-09 evening): the planner chose one pen roll per seam and capped the joint step between consecutive tacks at 69°; on C1's closed seam (4 tacks round the 62 mm pipe) it failed at the second tack (wrist would turn 115°); with a roll per tack (chosen by clearance up to 10 mm, then the smallest joint step) bench-like scenes reach every tack of C1, E2 and RR1 with 6.6–9.7 mm minimum clearance; lesson: per seam for lines, per tack for curves, each move between tacks left to the transit planner — TN 2026-10-09 (evening)
@S 3.9.2 | Geometric failures reported, not forced: SP3 4/6 (holder within 1.7 mm of the band at its concave lobes); S3 on R2 3/4 (the far side has no elbow-up solution) — TN 2026-10-09 (evening)
@S 3.9.2 | Cable wind-up (2026-10-16): each transit went to the 2π-equivalent of the next pose nearest the previous one (continuity), so wrist_3 kept winding; the same tool pose was available at +73° instead of −287°; every good contact (14 on E2 the day before, one on SP3) had wrist_3 within ±151° of home, the two false ones were the only tacks beyond ±180° (−202°, −287°); wrist_1 (to +139° on E2) and the elbow did not separate them; a ±180° window about home loses no pose and keeps the cable slack; after the fix the scene plans all four tacks with wrist_3 within −174°..0 — TN 2026-10-16 cable
@S 3.9.2 | Wrist wind-up (2026-09-29/10-01): after rolled tacks the home path arrived one wrist turn off (5.42 rad instead of −0.86); it now returns to the exact home joints — TN Earlier/motion-safety
@S 3.9.3 | Joint-space shortest is not what a cell should drive round a pipe (2026-10-14 afternoon): transits round a pipe swung the pen through loops up to 0.9 m off and the arm hit things 75 cm away; APS minimises weighted joint travel; nearly equal joint cost (3.5 vs 3.9 rad) had tip paths 257 vs 772 mm; repeated runs: 2 s budget 497–1,873 mm tip travel, 5 s 353–595 mm; more threads or parallel runs were worse (8 shared cores) — TN 2026-10-14 (afternoon)
@S 3.9.3 | Departure points: starting each transit 100 mm out along the pen instead of 35 mm made most transits consistent, but not all; the straight Cartesian line between departure points (the move a welder would program, tool sliding while it turns) came out at 141–211 mm, at the minimum joint cost, identically on every run; "A sampling planner only needs to step in when that line is blocked" — TN 2026-10-14 (afternoon)
@S 3.9.3 | Lessons: "Optimise the quantity you care about" (tool sweep, not joint length); "Structure first, then search" (departure points + Cartesian line common case, APS fallback); re-planning on every small deviation threw the good plan away — joining the planned path from where the arm actually stands kept it — TN 2026-10-14 (afternoon)
@S 3.9.3 | Planner margin incident (2026-10-14): most transits logged "Invalid start"/"Invalid goal" and fell back to a slow planner; the transit clearance is set to 90 % of an approach pose's own and the 2 mm safety margin on top made that pose invalid; each fallback took 1.5–3 min; margin capped + path checks on the C++ model: the same plan 17.6 s instead of 478 s; "Safety margins compose" — TN 2026-10-14
@S 3.9.3 | APS motivation (2026-10-06 evening): transits deviated because the planner took the first RRT-Connect path; OMPL's AnytimePathShortening (parallel planners + path hybridization + shortcutting) is the remedy, but its Python bindings do not exist and a Python collision callback would serialize its threads → the collision model ported to C++ (pybind11) — TN 2026-10-06 (evening)
@S 3.9.3 | Transit clearance (2026-09-29/10-01): with the tack clearance (3 mm) also used for free moves, a transit passed 3.7 mm from the ear and hit it at 9 N; free moves now keep 20 mm — TN Earlier/motion-safety
@S 3.9.4 | False contact (2026-10-16, SP3): two of four reachable tacks stopped in the air, "contact" at 1.5 N with the pen 66–68 mm above the part in both runs, 82–84 mm before the registered point (16–18 mm into the descent); the planned descent had no kink (waypoints 5 mm apart, joint-speed changes ≤ 0.004 rad/s in the fast leg) so the force grew slowly; a stroke after one read −1 N in the air; cause: the wound camera cable; "A force-gated descent senses everything that pulls on the tool, and the camera cable is part of the tool" — TN 2026-10-16 cable
@S 3.9.4 | Table refusal → overshoot rule (2026-10-16): root tack 4.9 mm above the table (5.5 mm plate), pen 3.5 mm above it at the tack point, 5 mm of overshoot along a pen 35° off vertical reached the table, and the marking node refused ("the pen reaches the table"); the reachability check does not look at the overshoot; rule now: no surface can lie beyond the measured table, so an overshoot that reaches it ends there instead of refusing the tack — TN 2026-10-16 cable
@S 3.9.4 | Every descent stops at a 1.5 N touch, so its tip is a point on the real part — TN 2026-10-14
@S 3.9.4 | Force-sensor drift: after a power cycle the F/T sensor read 7 N at rest and every first transit aborted; the bias is now re-measured before every free move — TN Earlier/motion-safety
@S 3.9.4 | Overshoot: with the camera placing surfaces ~2 mm high, a 45° approach must travel ~2.8 mm past the plan, right at the 3 mm overshoot; marking tests used `overshoot_m` 6 mm until the constant is calibrated out (2026-10-02) — TN 2026-10-02 depth, re-check
@S 3.9.4 | "A 20 mm stroke along the registered seam reports more error than the touch itself on tight curves (it climbs the real wall). Measure accuracy with dots, demonstrate with strokes" — TN 2026-10-14 (evening)
@S 3.10.1 | Silent failure (2026-10-09): the seam step never ran because a new module was missing from the package's explicit install list; every test imports from the source tree and passed; the launched node imports the installed package, logged one warning and fell back to the old point-cloud detector; now it fails a test — TN 2026-10-09 (night)
@S 3.10.2 | C++ collision model (pybind11) checked against the Python model rather than trusted: FK equal to 1e-12; `is_valid` identical on 8 800 random and near-contact configurations; distances equal to 1e-9; 590× faster; lesson: test the port where they are most likely to differ (bisected near the collision boundary); the test found a pybind11 output bug and one exact tie decided by the last bit — TN 2026-10-06 (evening)

@@DOC notes/todo.md (TD)
- **Covers:** open work for both projects, headed "(2026-10-08)" but edited through 2026-10-16: "Where we are" (the mode-A chain, marks 2.3–2.7 mm on 2026-10-06), the decisions (no touch sensing; FP tracking; APS transits), the curved-strata NEXT list with dated bench progress 2026-10-09..10-15, "Benchmarks to run (for the thesis)", "measure everything" (per-stage timing table), multi-view items (anchor the offset to the table, self-calibration bench check), accuracy small items, OPEN motion, OPEN perception (mode B, mode A+, quality field + DP, tack length, the professor's "walking points", laser pointer), OPEN tack ordering by heat simulation (2026-10-16), OPEN HRI (the camera aim: design, buzz diagnosis, relay), parts/data/paper (Phase 9, weld_generator items), LATER, and an undated "important" note (planned paths through the table on the real robot).
- **Status:** current (open items only, by its own rule); some items struck through as done (mode A curved; RViz loads PLY; 500 Hz smoothing). The heading date (2026-10-08) is older than the content. A few lines are older than later edits inside it (e.g. "tune ... the 4° limit" vs "`mv_max_correction_deg` raised to 5" on 2026-10-15).
- **Decisions (dated):** 2026-10-02 no touch sensing; 2026-10-02/06 FP tracking (`tracking_source: fp` default), desktop registers / laptop tracks, ICP keeps stationary work, no Kalman; 2026-10-06 `transit_planner: aps` default with RRT-Connect fallback; 2026-10-15 acceptance rules "apply a correction within the limits"; `mv_max_correction_deg` → 5 (2026-10-15); aim: velocity controller with own Jacobian chosen over MoveIt Servo; P loop not PID; mode B "probably dropped" (2026-10-15, not decided).
- **Feeds:** 3.4.2 (most complete design record of the aim), 3.5.2, 3.6, 3.8 (heat ordering, tack length), 3.9, 5.5, 6.3-6.9 (benchmarks), 7 (future work), Ch.4/Phase 9 links.
@@END
@S 3.1 | Where we are (2026-10-06): mode A runs end to end on the robot, by gestures; the pen marks every tack within 2.3–2.7 mm ("possibly within the measurement error"; ~8 mm on 24 Sep); chain: (1) calibrated kinematics, depth Tare'd against the robot, extrinsic tilt refined; (2) register on the desktop: SAM2 → PPF → FoundationPose; (3) track while moved: FP on the laptop's GPU (25–29 Hz); (4) save: robust mean of tracker poses at rest, then ICP on 5 live clouds; (5) `~/refine_pose`: 4 close views, online camera-offset correction, ISO 5817 fit-up check; (6) seams and tacks (mode A, D4 rule, tackrule-0.1); (7) reachability; transits by OMPL APS (C++), spline, TOTG; descents with a roll fallback; (8) force-gated touch; dot or stroke — TD §Where we are
@S 3.4.1 | Mode follows whether a frame is frozen: with the robot at home `Pointing_Up` is a live AIM mode; the first `ILoveYou` (capture) freezes the frame and hands over to the FP bridge and the dwell cursor; once frozen, `Pointing_Up` means the cursor again, "so no new gesture is needed" — TD §OPEN HRI (What)
@S 3.4.1 | Everyday start: `ros2 launch admittance_control gestures.launch.py` starts both nodes (`aim_dry_run:=true` only logs; `aim:=false` gestures without the aim); `gestures.launch.py` starts the relay; the aim node refuses to start without it — TD §OPEN HRI
@S 3.4.2 | Motion orientation only: the camera turns about its optical centre, position held; aiming takes 2 rotations (pan, tilt); roll about the optical axis is "the over-actuated one: hold it at the home value, so the image stays upright" — TD §OPEN HRI (Motion)
@S 3.4.2 | Control: IBVS, "a filtered P loop, not a full PID"; error = index fingertip pixel (MediaPipe landmark) minus image centre → angular velocity about camera x and y; gain, deadband (~5 % of the image), speed cap (~15°/s), acceleration/jerk limits; no integral term ("the camera-to-command latency (~70–100 ms) makes it wind up and overshoot"); no derivative ("the landmark is noisy"); landmark smoothed by a One-Euro filter at the 30 Hz frame rate — TD §OPEN HRI (Control)
@S 3.4.2 | Execution: a streaming interface, not the trajectory action; chosen: the driver's `forward_velocity_controller` with our own Jacobian solve (6×6, calibrated kinematics), needs no MoveIt Servo config and the collision model and branch lock run every cycle anyway; rejected: MoveIt Servo on `forward_position_controller` (own singularity/collision slowdown, Ruckig/Butterworth smoothing); controllers switched on entering/leaving AIM; `scaled_joint_trajectory_controller` must be active again before `refine_pose` and marking — TD §OPEN HRI (Execution)
@S 3.4.2 | Safety (design): deadman — no fingertip for 0.3 s → decelerate to a stop; any other gesture stops it too; excursion limits from home e.g. ±35° pan and ±25° tilt; every commanded pose checked with the C++ collision model (µs) and the branch lock; the pendant's speed slider still scales everything — TD §OPEN HRI (Safety)
@S 3.4.2 | After the aim: capture from the aimed pose, `T_base_cam` from TF at the image stamp; whether `refine_pose` and marking start from there or from `home_q` to decide (both plan from the current joints) — TD §OPEN HRI (After it)
@S 3.4.2 | Clock check: the image stamp and `/joint_states` must share a clock; if not, the node says so once ("latency not compensated") and still works with a little overshoot — TD §OPEN HRI (bench 3)
@S 3.4.2 | Built 2026-10-15: `admittance_control/camera_aim.py` (control law, 14 tests incl. a closed loop on the UR kinematics), `scripts/camera_aim_node.py`, `/gesture/aim` in the gesture node; tested against a fake controller manager and arm — TD §OPEN HRI
@S 3.4.2 | Buzz diagnosis details: bag `rosbag2_2026_10_16-13_49_03` 45 s, 32 s of aiming; "the 5 % deadband is 64 px ≈ 4°, and engaging jumps the target from 0 to ~8°/s (2.5 /s × 0.8 × 4°)"; ruled out: the I/D question (not the loop oscillating) and tiny commands (hold-still windows |cmd| < 0.002 rad/s as quiet as idle) — TD §OPEN HRI
@S 3.4.2 | Relay: the C++ `aim_rate_relay` takes the aim node's 125 Hz rates on `/camera_aim/joint_rates`, ramps linearly between them over the measured input period (8 ms of delay), streams to `forward_velocity_controller` at 500 Hz; alternatives if it disappointed: short spline segments to `scaled_joint_trajectory_controller`, or MoveIt Servo — TD §OPEN HRI (Fixes)
@S 3.5.2 | Re-registration fix: deploy the updated `scripts_in_foundationpose/fp_server.py` to the desktop (`object_name` on re-registration) — TD §NEXT step 5
@S 3.5.2 | FP small items: `use_roi` (off) — does the crop raise the rate?; symmetric parts (tubes) need `SYMMETRY_INFO` on the server or the pose may spin about the axis; RViz loads the PLY meshes for `/perception/fp/marker` (user, 2026-10-16) — TD §OPEN perception
@S 3.6 | Multi-view pending: FoundationPose per view as a second measurement (plan step 8); view-to-view transits on APS (`multiview.plan_views` still uses RRT-Connect); to try: viewing distance 0.32–0.35 m, a 30° elevation where reachable; tune `mv_max_relative_mm`, the 4° limit, `online_selfcal_min_mm`; keep 2 rounds (switch to one combined solve only if rounds oscillate or depend on order; watch `rounds_run`) — TD §Multi-view
