@@DOC notes/pipeline map.md + notes/pipeline_map.dot (PMAP, Turkish)
- **Covers:** the bubble diagram of the pipeline "from placing the part to the tack mark" (source `pipeline_map.dot`, renders `pipeline_map.png/.svg`) and a table per bubble: what it does, what it takes, what it gives (with array sizes). Clusters: PREPARATION (once: CAD library, calibration chain), the camera and the operator, the GPU SERVER (Docker, one frame per part: SAM2 → mask → cloud → PPF → FoundationPose), LAPTOP per part (live cloud, base transform, FP tracking, save), LAPTOP after all parts (close views, seams, tacks, reachability, motion + mark, validation).
- **Status:** last edited 2026-10-08, so it predates the curved parts (2026-10-09..), departure points and Cartesian transits (2026-10-14), true-shape neighbours (2026-10-14), spin settling (2026-10-15), the camera aim (2026-10-15), the relay and the cable window (2026-10-16); its "APS ... 1 s" is the pre-2026-10-14 budget (now 2 s, RM). Seams box says "plate" primitives only. Otherwise consistent with RM. The draft's Fig. mc_pipeline must be redrawn from it with those additions (ch3 L51 says "redraw in English").
- **Numbers held:** RGB 720×1280×3, depth 720×1280 (mm, aligned to colour), K 3×3; scene cloud N×6, N ≤ 2000; model clouds ~600×6; PPF: 12 pose hypotheses → ICP + score = coverage × explanation; FoundationPose ~252 orientation hypotheses; FP tracking 25–29 Hz, 70–90 ms; save-time ICP on 5 live clouds, accepted at fitness ≥ 0.2 and correction ≤ 20 mm / 8°; 4 views at 0.40 m, 45°/60°, 16-frame median; pose tolerance 10 mm; tack rule length 4t, spacing ≤ 33t (example: 8 mm plate, 250 mm seam → 2 tacks, 32 mm long); 6 Oct 2.3–2.7 mm.
- **Feeds:** Fig. mc_pipeline and fig:architecture (3.1, 3.10.1, ch1 fig:intro_pipeline), 3.5.1 (PPF details), 3.8 (tack example).
@@END
@S 3.1 | Pipeline map clusters and data (source for Fig. mc_pipeline): PREPARATION (once): CAD library (PLY mesh in mm, same file names on both computers, per CAD a PPF table + weld primitive) and the calibration chain (robot kinematics → pen tip → table plane → camera depth (Tare) → hand-eye rotation; reference = pen-tip touches); CAMERA (D435i on the wrist; RGB 720×1280×3 + depth 720×1280 mm aligned to colour + K 3×3); OPERATOR (click (u, v)); GPU SERVER (Docker, one frame per part): SAM2 (mask 720×1280, RGB only) → mask ⊙ depth back-projected with K, edge eroded, normals, downsampled → scene cloud N×6, N ≤ 2000 (m, camera frame) → PPF (model clouds ~600×6) → part name → FoundationPose (~252 hypotheses) → T_cam←part 4×4 (m); LAPTOP per part: base transform T_base←part = FK(q)·T_wrist←cam·T_cam←part; live organized cloud H×W×3; FP tracking (25–29 Hz, track_one, robot motion removed by TF, re-registration on the desktop without click); save (robust mean → ICP on 5 fresh live clouds with the normal gate, the table and the saved parts removed → pose_static); LAPTOP after all parts: close views (4 × 0.40 m, 16-frame medians, parts in turn, no interpenetration, camera offset, ISO 5817 warning) → seams (plane intersections, D4, pose tolerance 10 mm, "the seam is not searched in the image") → tacks (4t, ≤ 33t, end margin, order ends → middles, sides alternating) → reachability (tilt × roll per tack, same arm posture, arm/tool/parts/table collision-free, largest clearance) → motion and mark (straight path or APS: 4 parallel RRT-Connect, combined + shortened, C++, 1 s; spline + TOTG; if the descent collides at the report's roll, other rolls are tried; descent along the pen stops at 1.5 N; dot or line; retract) → validation (pen touches; 6 Oct 2.3–2.7 mm) — PMAP table, .dot
@S 3.5.1 | PPF as run on the server: pair features (1 distance + 3 angles, pose-invariant), scene pairs vote in each CAD's table, the 12 top-voted poses are refined by ICP and scored by coverage × explanation ("kapsama × açıklama"); the highest score wins; outputs part name, score table, margin — PMAP table "PPF: hangi parça?"
@S 3.5.1 | SAM2 uses only the RGB image; a negative click carves away the overflow; the masked depth is back-projected (X = (u−cx)·Z/fx, Y = (v−cy)·Z/fy), the mask edge eroded, normals computed, downsampled to N ≤ 2000 — on the GPU server — PMAP table
@S 3.5.2 | FoundationPose registration: ~252 orientation hypotheses, each rendered and compared with the real image and refined, the best-scored chosen — PMAP table
@S 3.5.3 | Save-time ICP: from the robust mean (median position, mean rotation, jumping frames discarded) ICP on 5 fresh live clouds with the normal gate ("the back face does not match"), the table and the already saved parts removed; used if fitness ≥ 0.2 and correction ≤ 20 mm / 8°; "tracking says where it is now, saving says where exactly" — PMAP table "Kaydet"
@S 3.8 | Tack example: 8 mm plate, 250 mm seam → 2 tacks, 32 mm long (rule: length 4t, spacing ≤ 33t, margin from the ends; t = the thinner part) — PMAP table "Puntalar"
@S 3.9.3 | Roll fallback at planning: "if the descent collides at the report's pen roll, the other rolls are tried" (marking node) — PMAP table "Hareket ve işaret"
@S 5.1 | Camera streams: RGB 720×1280×3 and depth 720×1280 (mm), depth aligned to colour pixels — PMAP table "Kamera"
@C L264-266 | clicks + frozen colour image → SAM2 → mask; masked depth → cloud — PMAP
@C L273-275 | best hypotheses verified by explanation and coverage — PMAP (12 hypotheses, ICP, score = coverage × explanation)
@X L276 | ch3 TODO "The verification score (coverage × explanation); 12 hypotheses" — confirmed by PMAP; add that the 12 top-voted poses are refined by ICP before scoring, and that the PPF pose is discarded (FP re-estimates, RM §12) — PMAP, RM §12
@M 3.1 | The pipeline map shows WHICH MACHINE runs each step (SAM2, mask→cloud, PPF and FP registration in Docker on the GPU server; everything else on the laptop) — use it for fig:architecture and fig:tracking_split; update it with the camera aim, curved seams, spin settling, departure points/Cartesian transits and the 2 s APS budget — 3.1 / 3.10 — PMAP
@@DOC notes/quality_field_k_point_selection.md (SMALL: quality field)
- **Covers:** an advice text (dated 2026-08-12 by git; written as a reply from an assistant: "I was wrong to reach for greedy submodular selection") on robot-aware tack placement: Stage 1 a quality field over arc length s × pen roll φ (nominal orientation from Yi et al. and Wang et al.; IK feasibility gate, σ_min(J), force transmission along the contact normal, joint-limit margin, camera terms, collision clearance; normalised to [0,1]); collapsing φ by a shortest path with a transition penalty; Stage 2 selecting k tacks by an exact 1-D dynamic programme with spacing constraints; choosing k; where the distortion literature enters; "the experiment that decides everything".
- **Status:** NOT BUILT (RM L128 "the thesis' quality field / tack selection, not built yet"; TD "Quality field and DP tack selection (thesis stages 1–3)" open; TP open decision 1). Its "collapsing φ" DP is close to what the cell does for a curved seam's roll (CS step 3: joint travel + 0.15 rad per mm of clearance under 10 mm).
- **Feeds:** 3.8 (open decision), 3.9.2 (the roll DP's lineage), 7 (future work), ch1 contributions (decision with the advisor).
@@END
@S 3.8 | Robot-aware placement idea (not built): R(s, φ) = R_nom(s)·Rot_z(φ), R_nom from the literature: Yi et al. weld direction d_weld = n1 × n2 and approach v0 = v1 + v2 (dihedral bisector); Wang et al. Z_W = −(n_b + n_m); field terms at each (s, φ): IK feasibility (hard gate; UR5e closed-form IK has 8 branches, record which), σ_min(J) (not |det J|: mixes m and rad), force transmission along the contact normal 1/√(nᵀ(J_v J_vᵀ)n) ("the dual of the velocity ellipsoid ... the wrong one for contact"), joint-limit margin, camera terms (frustum, standoff in the valid depth range, incidence, self-occlusion by the holder; check the minimum range first), collision clearance (the camera body is the widest geometry; the vertical plate restricts the approach cone); each normalised to [0,1] — quality_field_k_point_selection.md §Stage 1
@S 3.8 | Grid: s at 2 mm over 232 mm (116 samples) × φ at 5° (72) ≈ 8,400 poses, closed-form IK + Jacobian each, vectorised NumPy "well under a second"; collapse φ with a shortest path over (s, φ) with transition penalty μ|Δφ| and a hard block on IK-branch changes (exact small DP; smooth roll profile; for discrete tacks continuity matters less but branch consistency still does) — quality_field_k_point_selection.md §Stage 1
@S 3.8 | Stage 2: candidates s1 < … < sn; hard constraints: both endpoints mandatory, spacing d_min ≤ Δs ≤ d_max, no tack within ~2t of a free edge, IK feasible; f(i,j) = q(s_i) + max_m [f(m, j−1) − λ g(s_m, s_i)] with g penalising non-uniform spacing (pairwise, so it stays inside the recursion); O(n²k) (n = 116, k ≤ 15 → ~200k operations, milliseconds, global optimum); k by sweep or a fixed cost c per tack; k_min = ⌈L/d_max⌉ + 1; Tomków's measurement that five tacks gave higher angular distortion than fewer argues for a real penalty on k; the distortion literature enters only through d_min, d_max, the edge margin and c ("no FEM") — quality_field_k_point_selection.md §Stage 2
@S 3.8 | The deciding experiment: plot q across the seam before writing selection code; if σ_min and force transmission vary < ~10 % over a 232 mm seam, the kinematic part has no signal; expectation: camera visibility and collision carry most of the variation → framing "visibility-and-reachability-aware"; measure the feasible φ range against the vertical plate early (a 5° grid may be too coarse) — quality_field_k_point_selection.md §The experiment
@O 3.8 | Robot-aware tack placement (quality field + exact DP for k tacks) — NOT BUILT; TP decision 1 ("the thesis core" per the handoff) — decide with the advisor: build it (a section + results) or future work — quality_field_k_point_selection.md, TP, TD
@M 3.9.2 | The roll DP of 3.9.2 is the "collapsing φ" shortest path of the quality-field note restricted to clearance + joint travel: say so (it also answers ch3 L489-491's note) — 3.9.2 / 3.8 — quality_field_k_point_selection.md, CS
@M 2.1 | Tomków (tack count vs angular distortion) is cited in the quality-field note as the argument for penalising k — check the citation for 2.1/3.8 — quality_field_k_point_selection.md
@@DOC notes/realtime_icp.md (RI, 2026-07-06)
- **Covers:** the ICP tracking methodology: Phase 1 initialisation (SAM-6D detection, mask crop, pose T0, CAD at T0 → `current_pose`) and Phase 2 loop per frame (dynamic CropBox replaces the mask; Anderson-accelerated point-to-plane ICP with the Welsch metric ignores intruding grippers/fixtures; update state; RViz); lost-tracking safeguard (fitness below e.g. 0.1 → pause, re-run Phase 1); the Fast-Robust-ICP repository.
- **Status:** historical/superseded for the default path (FP tracking since 2026-10-06; SAM-6D replaced by FoundationPose); still the methodology of `tracking_source:=icp` (RM §7).
- **Feeds:** 3.5.2 (why it was replaced), 2.5 (robust/accelerated ICP), App. C.
@@END
@S 3.5.2 | Historical ICP tracker idea (2026-07-06): "the output of frame N−1 acts as the input for frame N"; CropBox from the object's dimensions plus a margin replaces the SAM-6D mask ("90 % of the factory background is deleted"); Welsch weights make intruding grippers/fixtures (> 3ν) negligible; Anderson acceleration for rate; lost if fitness < e.g. 0.1 → re-run SAM-6D — RI
@@DOC notes/ground_removal.md (2026-07-09)
- **Covers:** absolute Z-truncation in `base_link` (the bin-picking baseline): delete points below the known bench height + threshold; < 0.1 ms; "the algorithm physically cannot see the table".
- **Status:** superseded on 2026-09-28: the flat cut at −100 mm left the tilted table (1.57°) up to 6 mm above the cut; the cut now follows the pen-measured plane (RM §14 28 Sep row).
- **Feeds:** 3.3.1 (the incident), 3.5.3 (scene filtering).
@@END
@S 3.3.1 | The original ground removal assumed a level bench ("You know the exact Z-height of your workbench") — the measured tilt (1.57°) made the flat cut fail on 2026-09-28 — ground_removal.md, RM §14
@@DOC notes/model_based_background.md (2026-07-09)
- **Covers:** model-based background subtraction ("digital twin masking"): the Static Environment Point Cloud (SEPC) built from each saved part's CAD at its final pose; live points within a noise threshold (e.g. < 5 mm) of the SEPC deleted before ICP, so a new part pushed flush against a saved one is tracked alone.
- **Status:** current concept (`bg_subtract_dist_m`, RM §7/§D); numbers illustrative.
- **Feeds:** 3.5.3 (the static environment), the fact check of ch3 L309-310.
@@END
@S 3.5.3 | SEPC rationale: if object B gets within a few cm of A, A's points enter B's CropBox and the KD-tree matches B's CAD to A's surface; "You already know the exact pose of the fixed objects" → delete live points within a noise threshold (e.g. < 5 mm) of the SEPC, then voxel (~50k → ~2k points), normals, Fast ICP; "Object A literally disappears from the point cloud that your ICP algorithm sees" — model_based_background.md
@X L309-310 | (supporting RM) "the static environment that the next part is registered against": model_based_background.md and PMAP ("the table and the saved parts are removed" before the save-time ICP) describe the SEPC as SUBTRACTED from the scene — model_based_background.md, PMAP

@@DOC notebooks/idea.md (IDEA, 2026-05-13/14, Turkish)
- **Covers:** the earliest plan: use a SAM3 mask to find a plate's boundary (OpenCV contours), generate random 3-D points inside it at least 15 px from the edge (`cv2.pointPolygonTest`, 3×3 median depth, back-projection with a fixed T_base_to_cam), then probe them with the robot by RTDE (moveL to 5 cm above, speedL −5 mm/s down, stop at |F_z| > 2.0 N, record the TCP, retract 10 mm) and fit a plane.
- **Status:** historical (fixed camera, touch probing, SAM3); the project later chose vision-only localisation (TN 2026-10-02); the pen touch-off of the table descends from it.
- **Feeds:** only the project history (ch1 motivation, if wanted); 3.3.1 (touching the table with force-stopped probes).
@@END

@@END
@S 3.0 | Author's question: "should we mention no touch sensing" before 3.1 — the docs support stating it with its rationale (TN 2026-10-02: "a scenario where the parts cannot be touched must remain valid"; touches only for calibration and independent measurement) — CAGO, TN
@S 3.1 | Author's wording: the system finds the location and pose of each part and computes where they meet; the pen stands in for the torch, so reach, accuracy and geometry must be respected, but heat is not modelled (future work: heat "as a parameter", not a heat model) — CAGO §3.1
@S 3.2 | Notation the author wants in a table: parts k = 1..K; FK ᴮT_E(q); the set of weldable seams S; points p_i; pen axes a_i; the collision-free joint trajectory bringing the pen tip to p_i along a_i — CAGO §3.2, TP §Notation (frames B, E, C, O)
@S 3.4.2 | Author's rationale for aiming with only 2 rotations: the gesture recognition knows the bottom and top of the image — matches RM §E "the gesture classifier is deliberately orientation-dependent ... present the hand upright relative to the image" (holding the roll at home keeps the image upright for the classifier and the operator) — CAGO §3.4.2, RM §E
@S 3.5.2 | Machines: registration on a separate machine with an NVIDIA RTX 5070 Ti; tracking and all other processes on a machine with an RTX 4060 mobile GPU (TN/RF: "the laptop's 8 GB RTX 4060") — CAGO §3.5.2, TN 2026-10-06
@M 3.1 | CAGO asks for the pipeline figure in 3.1 and a notation table in 3.2 (both are placeholders/TODOs in ch3) — 3.1/3.2 — CAGO
@@DOC notes/curved_seams_plan.md (CS)
- **Covers:** mode A for the curved strata (written 2026-10-09, edited to 2026-10-15): the idea (WeldSet's verification arm `verify_curved.rediscover_seam` used forward: seams = intersections of the registered parts' analytic surfaces); the stratum → seam → weld_generator constructor table; steps 1–5 with their status: (1) registry entries for the printed parts (fits and verification table), (2) the curved branch of `compute_seams` (pair table, end detection, fit-up, per-point frames, verdicts, closed-seam tacks), (3) reachability per tack, collision boxes for curved parts, curved strokes, (4) the bench: perception only (10-09) with PPF margins and three silent failures, then marks (10-14) with contact analysis, the planner-margin and table-in-model fixes, transits round a pipe (departure points, Cartesian line, joined paths, DP rolls), RR1, SP3, S3 on R2, the step-4 summary table, (5) multi-view with true shapes (synthetic table), the stale-registry incident, SP3 and E2 with multi-view (10-15), the acceptance-rule changes, the cut-pipe spin settling and E2's result.
- **Status:** current and the most detailed record for 3.5.4, 3.7, 3.9 and 6.8. Superseded inside it: the step-3 "S3 on R2 3/4 (far side no elbow-up)" scene is marked INVALID (found 2026-10-14: S3 hung under R2; the real bench reached 4/4) — TN 2026-10-09 (evening) and TD still carry the invalid 3/4; the "transit_budget_s (now 2 s, as asked)" line was overtaken the same afternoon by "APS: transit_budget_s 3 s" (code default 3.0 s, `tack_marking_node.py` L113, "1.0 before 2026-10-14"); the butt arc (BA) row of the idea table was never built (step 2's done table has no slab–slab arc rule).
- **Decisions (dated):** registry fits box → tube → extrusion, first that verifies wins, both ways at the 0.25 mm budget (10-09); bore_min_diameter_mm 200 for the pen holder + camera (10-09); roll/tilt per tack on curved seams (10-09); 6 rotated boxes per pipe, sector boxes for cut pipes, box chains for bands (10-09); departure points 100 mm, Cartesian line after the joint edge and before APS, bows 30/60/100 mm, drive the planned path, APS 3 s, exact DP for the rolls (10-14 afternoon); measure in `stroke_mode:=dot` (10-14); true-shape neighbours (10-14); stale registry entries caught (10-15); `mv_max_correction_deg` 4 → 5 (10-15); acceptance: bore hollow, overlap judged against the saved assembly, pipe spin not judged (10-15); pen may touch the part AND the table at a tack (10-15); `settle_cut_spins` in `welding_points` (10-15).
- **Feeds:** 3.5.4, 3.6.2, 3.6.5, 3.7.1–3.7.4, 3.8, 3.9.1–3.9.4, 5.3, 5.4, 5.5, 6.3, 6.6, 6.7, 6.8.
@@END
@S 3.7 | Idea: WeldSet draws the seam curve first and builds the parts around it (D29, curve-first); to check itself `verify_curved.rediscover_seam` recomputes the seam from the placed parts alone (poses and radii, never the drawn curve); "The cell has exactly that input" — seam = intersection of the registered parts' analytic surfaces, "with no seam detection in the point cloud" — CS §The idea
@S 3.7.3 | Stratum → seam → constructor: T/circle (C1), T/ellipse (E2): plate top plane ∩ tube outer cylinder, a circle or an ellipse if tilted (`curves.ellipse_from_plane_cylinder`); T/saddle (S3 on R2): branch ∩ run cylinder (`curves.saddle_from_cylinders`); T/rounded_rect (RR1): the tube's outer outline (closed spine) laid onto the plate (`SweptSlab` spine, `rounded_rect_curve`); T/swept_path (SP3): the band's two side faces on the plate, spine offset ± t/2 (`OffsetCurve`); butt arc (BA, laser-cut MDF): two matching arc edges (`Arc3D`) — planned, not in the built pair table — CS §The idea, step 2
@S 3.7.3 | Why curved seams tolerate registration error: a plane ∩ cylinder curve does not change if the tube slides along its own axis or sits a few mm above/inside the plate; the gap/penetration between the tube's cut end and the plate along the curve is the fit-up value — CS §The idea
@S 3.7.3 | Built pair table (step 2, 2026-10-09, `curved_seams.py`, `compute_seams` → `pair_seams`): tube on a plate (C1, E2, R2, S3 on its flat end): plate plane ∩ outer wall, circle or ellipse, closed; tube on a tube (S3 on R2): the saddle, closed; band on a plate (RR1, SP3): each wall traced along the band's axis onto the plate, closed outline = weld + bore, open band = two fillets; anything else (band on a pipe, two bands …): one non-weldable record "no seam rule for a X-Y pair yet" — CS step 2
@S 3.7.3 | "Which end stands on the other part is found, not assumed": both ends and both plate faces tried; an end counts when its distance to the curve along the member's axis stays within `pose_tol_mm` all round, and the member must stand at least 10° off the face — CS step 2
@S 3.7.3 | Per-point frames: `n_a_per_point` (base), `n_b_per_point` (wall), `approach_per_point` (bisector), `dihedral_deg_range`: C1 90°; E2 65–115°; saddle 90° at the crown, 120° at the flanks; verdict = weldgen's `seam_verdict`: the torch cone at every point, weldable at ≥ 95 % clear; bores are negatives unless the tool fits (`bore_min_diameter_mm` = 200 for the pen holder and camera; weldgen's 80 is a torch's); a curve running off the plate is `off_plate_edge`; per-point frames and verdicts come from weldgen `verify_curved.curved_seam_set` and `cone_clear_fractions` — CS step 2, §The idea
@S 3.7.3 | `runtime_access` takes the sheet-thickness cap from flat parts only (a tube wall is not a sheet); bench-like scenes compute in 0.1–0.8 s (C1, E2, RR1, S3 on R2) — CS step 2
@S 3.7.4 | Curved fit-up: `fitup_mm = {member: [min, max]}`, positive = gap, measured along the curve; a 3° tilt shows as a 0.4–3.6 mm gap range on C1, not a different seam; near misses: within 2× the tolerance a pair is reported, rejected, as `fitup_beyond_pose_tol (lo..hi mm)` (S3 on R2 replay: gap −5.3 to +10.5 mm vs 10 mm; S3's axis 7 mm to the side of R2's); SP3 on 2026-10-15 at −0.9..10.4 mm = "a near miss at 10 mm, weldable at 15 mm" — CS step 2, step 4 item 4, step 5
@S 3.7.4 | S3's saddle is cut at r 51.04, not R2's 50: seated on R2 it touches along the crown, the gap opens only ~0.15 mm at the saddle's flanks; placed coaxially as designed, step 2 reports 1.10–1.27 mm along the branch axis — CS step 1 findings
@S 3.7.4 | A pipe's fit-up is the least reliable number (a wrong spin turns the mitre's low side) but its seam is not moved by it (E2 re-run: fit-up −7.6..3.4 mm) — CS E2 re-run 2026-10-15
@S 3.7.1 | Registry (step 1, 2026-10-09): `tube` for C1, E2, R2, S3 with the base cut (flat, plane = mitre, or a cylinder = saddle against R2); `swept_slab` for RR1 (closed rounded-rect spine, band [0, wall]) and SP3 (open spine, band ± t/2); each entry has its parameters and `T_cad_prim`; derived from the mesh (`derive_tube`/`derive_extrusion`): axis from the lateral faces' normals, radii from the radial distances, cut surfaces fitted to the end faces, spines fitted to the band's centre surface; verified both ways (CAD vertices → primitive; primitive mesh → CAD surface, "which catches an entry that is too long"); accepted at the D34 chord budget; fits run box → tube → extrusion, the first that verifies wins — CS step 1, Status
@S 3.7.1 | Spine fit: the cap outline splits at its four 90° corners into side A, side B and two ends; side A, side B and the midline each fitted with a cubic (weldgen's clamped uniform knots), control points and point parameters solved together, control-point count raised until within 0.025 mm; side A won with 4 control points = the drawn curve (the 5-point sketch spline is the same curve, its middle three control points collinear); needs a good start (linear fit alternated with re-projection; from chord-length parameters it stalls at 0.06 mm); error = the solver's residual (a nearest-dense-sample measure added up to 0.1 mm); meshed spline side faces are up to 2.7° off the extrusion axis, so the extrusion test trusts the caps — CS step 1 findings
@S 3.7.1 | Two flat ends: C1, R2, RR1 can stand on either end; the entry puts the base at the lower end along the axis (RR1: at CAD y = −100); step 2 tries both — CS step 1 findings
@S 3.7.1 | Stale registry (2026-10-15): the plate CAD had been replaced (`test_objv2_base.ply` now 220 × 220 × 5.5 mm, thickness along CAD z; the registry still held the old 256 × 249 × 8 plate, thickness along y); mode A put the old shape at the new pose turned 90°, no seam record at all; `refine_pose` judged SP3 against the wrong plate and kept both as saved (SP3's 4.5° > the 4° limit); fixes: registry rebuilt; a stale entry is caught — a registered CAD's vertices must lie within 0.5 mm of its primitive (they do at 0.00–0.07 mm), else `welding_points` refuses and names `build_weldgen_registry.py --verify` and `refine_pose` judges that part by its real mesh's box with a WARNING; tests use frozen bench CADs (`test/data/bench_models/`) — CS step 5, SP3 first try
@S 3.5.4 | ICP at save refused on the pipes (2026-10-09): R2 5.7 mm / 30.8°, C1 8.5 mm / 9.0° against the 8° gate; "A flat-ended pipe looks the same at any spin about its own axis, so that spin is arbitrary"; fix: `pose_stats.remove_twist` keeps the tracker's spin and applies and gates only the axis tilt; used for uncut tubes only (`weldgen_registry.symmetry_axis`; "a cut end fixes the spin") — CS step 4 item 3
@S 3.5.4 | In multi-view a flat-ended pipe's spin is named "spin about its own axis (symmetric)" and kept out of the relative-pose rule (no fit-up depends on it); the prior holds it — CS step 5
@S 3.5.4 | Cut-pipe spin (2026-10-15, E2, another placement): no seam after multi-view; spin ~36° wrong (axis 25.2° to the plate, right for its 25° mitre; cut plane 15.4° off the plate; gap −14.8..+11.5 mm, a 26 mm range); single-view registration got it wrong and multi-view only added 3°; fix `curved_seams.settle_cut_spins`, run by `welding_points` before the seams: a pipe with a cut end standing on a part is turned about its own axis until the cut fits the base best (a 1-D search; the seam curve does not depend on the spin); accepted when the gap range is then ≤ 1.5 mm (or a quarter of before) and at least 1 mm better; the settled pose goes into `assembly.json` and the part's SEPC points (reachability and marking use it); on that assembly +36.25°, gap range 26.2 → 0.36 mm, one weldable seam (E2 1.5–1.8 mm into the plate, evenly); not done: settling inside `refine_pose` — CS E2 another placement
@S 3.6.2 | True shapes (step 5, built 2026-10-14): `part_shapes.py` gives each registered part a signed distance to its real surface from its primitive (a slab: its box; a pipe: its cylinder with the mitre or saddle cut, the bore solid → hollow for the overlap rule since 2026-10-15; a band: its walls, RR1's interior free); checked against every printed CAD: 0 mm on each vertex, normals out; used for the overlap rule, the contact samples and the fit-up gaps; a part without a registry entry keeps its box; the view planner gets the near-exact collision boxes (6 per pipe, a chain per band) for targets, occlusion and its collision model (SP3's bounding box had hidden the plate along the band) — CS step 5
@S 3.6.5 | E2 with multi-view (2026-10-15) refused for "overlap" (plate 4.3 mm, E2 2.6 mm) although both corrections were within 10 mm / 5°; the user's rule "a correction within the limits is applied"; three faults fixed: (1) a pipe's bore counted as solid (the plate under E2's bore read 4.3 mm inside E2) → hollow (planner boxes stay solid); (2) the overlap rule ignored the saved assembly → "an overlap over 1 mm refuses only a refinement that makes it WORSE than the saved poses; otherwise it is applied with a warning"; (3) a pipe's spin counted against the rotation limit (E2's 5.13° = 5.08° spin + 0.71° tilt) → the limit judges the axis tilt and the spin is reported — CS E2 with multi-view
@S 3.6.5 | Replayed after the fix: both parts accepted; the seam moves 4.5–5.3 mm mostly downwards: the refinement puts the plate flush on the touched table where the saved plate floated 2.7–6.4 mm above it, tilted; the pen's four contacts were all on E2's wall 5.8–7.9 mm above a table-flush plate (the plate lies directly on the table: confirmed); round a 25°-tilted tube, plate height and sideways offset trade off and four contacts cannot separate them (the free fit chose "plate +1.5 mm, E2 4.5 mm sideways"); `contact_error.py --plate-on-table` fixes the plate height from the touched table — CS E2 with multi-view
@S 3.8 | Closed-seam tacks: `compute_tacks` passes `closed` to `tackrule-0.1` ("an even count around a loop, phase from the scene id"); each tack's approach is interpolated from the per-point axes at its arclength; round C1 they rotate at 45° (4 tacks); `marking.visit_order` marks only the reachable tacks — CS step 2, step 3, §The idea (tacks.py)
@S 3.8 | Tack length on C1: a 12 mm tack (C1 wall 3 mm is the thinnest member → 4t = 12 mm) — CS step 3 strokes ("The chord p0→p1 missed C1 by 0.6 mm at a 12 mm tack")
@S 3.9.1 | Curved parts in the collision model (still boxes, so the C++ planner is unchanged): pipe = 6 boxes rotated about the axis, union covers the disk and stands at most 3.3 % of r outside (1.0 mm on C1; the single box stood 41 % proud); cut pipe (E2, S3) = radial half-box sectors each starting at its own lowest cut point (E2: 33, S3: 23), 0.2 mm outside the wall and hanging at most 4.5 mm below the cut under the wall (inside the 8 mm plate or inside R2); band = a chain of boxes along the spine within 1 mm (RR1: 9, SP3: 12); a pipe's boxes are solid (bore included), a band's follow its wall; before, `boxes_from_parts` boxed a tube about its base frame, half its length off — CS step 3, step 2
@S 3.9.1 | Table in the model (fixed 2026-10-14): `marking.json` had `table_z_m: null`, so transits could run through the table; now the collision model (Python and C++) takes the touched, tilted plane of `notebooks/table_plane.json` by default: −96.1 mm at its centroid, 1.70°, about −99.8 mm under the bench parts; fixtures in `marking.json` `fixtures` (3–4 pen-tip points on a fixture's top become a box standing on the table) — CS step 4 motion
@S 3.9.1 | Allowed contacts at a tack: the pen may touch the part AND the table (`tack_reach.pen_contact_pair`; at or below the table it is still a collision) — added 2026-10-15 after "pen × table 2.5 mm" refused a tack on the 5.5 mm plate lying on the table (re-run 4/4, min clearance 6.9 mm) — CS E2 re-run
@S 3.9.2 | Per-tack planning on curved seams (2026-10-09): one roll per seam with ≤ 69° joint step failed C1 at tack 2 (115°); `tack_reach._plan_per_tack` solves every tack on its own: best (tilt, roll) by clearance up to 10 mm, then the smallest joint step from the previous tack; unreachable tacks reported with their reason; the step limit does not apply (each tack has its own transit); the seam reports `n_reachable/n_tacks` — CS step 3
@S 3.9.2 | Rolls chosen together (2026-10-14 afternoon): "an exact DP minimising the weighted joint travel plus 0.15 rad per mm of clearance under 10 mm"; in the transit table "rolls chosen together (DP): endpoints within a few degrees of before; the gaps are geometric" (it did not fix the round-pipe loops) — CS §Transits round a pipe
@S 3.9.2 | The unreachable tacks are the expected kinds: a concave fillet tighter than the pen holder (SP3: holder 1.7–1.8 mm from the band in bench-like scenes, 0.3/0.9 mm on the bench); "Turning the part by hand, or a slimmer holder, would reach them"; reach planning 14–77 s per part offline (`tack_reachability.py`) — CS step 3, SP3 2026-10-14
@S 3.9.3 | Planner failure (2026-10-14): "Invalid start / goal" on most transits, not the time budget: `transit_model` sets the clearance to 90 % of an approach pose's own and APS's 2 mm margin on top made that pose invalid, so OMPL's threads never started; the Python RRT-Connect fallback took 1.5–3 min per transit round E2's 34 boxes, and the re-checks, smoothing and TOTG samples ran in Python; fixes: margin capped at half of the end poses' room; past `transit_budget_s` APS waits for a first path up to `transit_max_budget_s` 10 s; the C++ RRT-Connect before the Python one; re-checks on the C++ model (`marking.fast_is_valid`, ~590×); E2's plan 478 s → 17.6 s — CS step 4 motion
@S 3.9.3 | Transits round a pipe (2026-10-14 afternoon): E2 tack 2 → 3 transit: 102 mm tip chord, 1,977 mm tip path, 637 mm off the chord, the base joint 170° for a 68° change; fixes: departure points (`departure_m` 100 mm; a step whose departure segment is not clear keeps the 35 mm point, `departure_note`); "The tool's own straight line (`marking.cartesian_transit`) is tried after the straight joint edge and before APS. It is an IK chain on the locked branch, every pose and edge checked; when the chord passes too near the pipe it is bowed outward by 30, 60 or 100 mm"; the planned transit is driven (`_join_planned`) instead of re-planned; APS `transit_budget_s` 3 s "only for transits the first two cannot do"; E2's whole plan replayed: 7 s, tip paths 400 (from home, straight), 211, 191, 135 mm (478 s before) — CS §Transits round a pipe
@S 3.9.3 | RR1 (2026-10-14): all four transits were Cartesian lines or straight edges, planned paths joined and driven ("the path is amazing"); E2 15 Oct: every transit a Cartesian line or a straight edge — CS RR1, E2 result
@S 3.9.4 | Descent profile (2026-10-14): from the departure point fast (`v_approach_m_s` 0.05 m/s) to the 35 mm approach point, then 20 mm/s, the last 6 mm at 2 mm/s; the retract goes back out to the departure point — CS §Transits round a pipe
@S 3.9.4 | Strokes on curved seams: in `tack` mode a tack draws its own piece of the curve (`marking.seam_section`; the chord p0→p1 missed C1 by 0.6 mm at a 12 mm tack); in `seam` mode a curved seam also draws per-tack pieces ("one pen axis cannot draw a loop round a pipe"); `stroke_modes` tells the node so the once-per-seam skip does not drop the loop's other tacks; strokes still stop on registration error ("pen lifted off", once over 8 N); a `tack` stroke runs 20 mm along the REGISTERED seam after the contact and climbs the real wall round SP3's 23 mm bends; "For accuracy numbers, mark in `stroke_mode:=dot`" — CS step 3, RR1, SP3
@S 5.3 | Registry fits of the printed parts (2026-10-09): C1 tube flat base r 31.000, wall 3.000, length 100 (0.048 / 0.010 mm, 0.17 %); E2 tube plane cut (mitre 25.0°) r 45.003, wall 5.006, length 110 (0.049 / 0.019 mm, 0.00 %); R2 tube flat r 50.000, wall 6.000, length 180 (0.049 / 0.016 mm, 0.09 %); S3 tube saddle cut r 25.009, wall 4.018, length 99.34, cut r 51.04 with its axis at 70.0° to the branch (0.057 / 0.026 mm, 0.24 %); RR1 swept_slab closed rounded rect 64 × 64, corner r 12, band [0, 3], height 100 (0.037 / 0.004 mm, 0.11 %); SP3 (redrawn) open band, cubic spine with 4 control points (0, −110), (−200, 0), (200, 0), (0, 110), band [0, 5.00], height 80 (0.050 / 0.047 mm, 0.00 %); all far inside the 0.25 mm budget (chord error left); `270circle` also a spline band (0.067 / 0.041 mm); `vplate` (sharp kink) refused — CS Status step 1
@S 5.3 | Bench assemblies: C1 and E2 on the 8 mm plate (2026-10-14), E2 on "the new plate" (5.5 mm, 2026-10-15); S3 on R2 held by two V-blocks; RR1 and SP3 on the plate; the first SP3 (second side shifted 5 mm along CAD x, wall 2.4–5 mm) refused and reprinted the same day with a constant 5.00 mm wall (FreeCAD would not offset the spline) — CS step 1, step 4
@S 5.4 | Contact analysis method (`contact_error.py`): every descent stops at the touch so its tip is a point on the real part; fit "real tube = registered tube moved sideways, real plate = registered plate moved up/down" (rms 0.48 mm on E2); with a ~45° pen a sideways error puts one side's tack up the wall and the other near the root; the fit put the marks at 10.2/8.7 mm (wall side) and 4.0/3.3 mm (plate side) vs the ruler; pair sums match (14.2 vs 15.6, 12.0 vs 11.7), the split differs by ~3 mm (no tilt in the fit; the 20 mm stroke drifts after the contact); the error direction was not always the camera's line of sight (C1 mostly along it, E2 across it), "so it is not a plain depth offset"; a contact at the registered root only means a real surface passes there, "so the ruler is the number here"; `contact_error.py` fits tubes only; flags fits with large residuals (a tilted/spun registration) — CS step 4 marks, S3 on R2
@S 5.5 | Bench procedure per joint (curved): `~/reset_environment`, save the base, then the part, move nothing afterwards; `refine_pose` (Fist held 2 s) → `welding_points` (Palm) → `tack_reachability.py` → marking `~/plan` (dry run) then `~/all` in `stroke_mode:=dot`; measure every mark's offset from the real root (compare with the plate T's 2.3–2.7 mm); restart the launch after `colcon build`; every marking run kept in `marks_history/` with its assembly (C1's contacts had been overwritten by E2's run) — CS step 4, Bench (next)
@R 6.2 | First curved bench perception (2026-10-09; capture → SAM2 → PPF → FP → tracking → save): PPF margin over the runner-up C1 0.285 / 0.379 (over RR1), E2 0.386 (over smallplate), RR1 0.103 (over C1), R2 0.040 (over vplate, the thinnest), S3 named twice (scores 105, 108); tracker LOST: C1 once (fit 0.32), E2 (fit 0.12), RR1 (fit 0.49); S3 saved with ICP 1.1 mm / 1.3°; ICP at save refused R2 5.7 mm / 30.8°, C1 8.5 mm / 9.0° (8° gate); single session — CS step 4 first bench
@R 6.2 | Save-time ICP on the 2026-10-14 curved runs moved C1 13.1 mm (rmse 3.6 mm) and E2 7.6 mm (rmse 4.1 mm); "Whether it helped is not knowable from these runs" — CS step 4 marks
@R 6.3 | Synthetic true shapes vs bounding boxes (2026-10-14): C1 8 mm off sideways → accepted 0.08 mm (boxes the same); E2 on its mitre 5 mm off → 0.03 mm (boxes: plate pushed 49.7 mm, both refused, E2 left 5 mm off); S3 on R2 1.8 mm off → accepted 0.08 mm, no penetration (boxes: both refused, "overlaps by 1.9 / 2.0 mm"); RR1 3 mm off → accepted < 1 mm; view plan round the real C1 (archived 14 Oct): 4 views at azimuths 60/150/270/330; "So C1's 8.8 mm needed only `refine_pose`" — CS step 5
@R 6.3 | E2 re-run with the new rules (2026-10-15): `refine_pose` accepted both parts (E2 1.95 mm / 3.41° tilt+spin beyond the camera error, 3.4° of it spin); fit-up −7.6..3.4 mm; contacts above the table-flush plate top saved → refined: tack 4 6.4 → 2.4 mm, tack 1 5.8 → 5.4 mm (only 2 tacks marked) — CS E2 re-run
@R 6.6 | Offline E2 transit 2 → 3 study (2026-10-14; joint cost / tip path / max off the chord): APS 2 s from 35 mm approach points 3.7–11.0 / 497–1,873 mm / 234–879 mm; APS 3 s 3.0–5.2 / 340–743 / 134–368; APS 5 s 3.0–3.2 / 353–595 / 149–254; APS 3 s with 8 threads 3.7–6.0 / 410–1,036 / worse; 3 parallel APS runs 3.6–8.6 each / 263–942 kept / the runs compete for the cores; APS 3 s from 100 mm departure points 3.02–3.06 / 194–206 / 39–55 mm (but 1 → 2: 432–1,623 mm); rolls by DP: endpoints within a few degrees; straight Cartesian move between departure points 3.35 / 3.02 / 1.14 / 211 / 191 / 141 mm, the same every run; the original transit: 102 mm chord, 1,977 mm tip path, 637 mm off, base joint 170° for 68°; repeated runs (N not stated) — CS §Transits round a pipe

@@DOC notes/seam_two_modes_plan.md (S2, 2026-09-21)
- **Covers:** the plan of record that replaced the radius-PCA seam: the finding that `~/welding_points` runs on CAD clouds at registered poses, so the seam is implied by the poses (mode A computes, does not detect); mode A (registry, `seam_from_registration`, pose tolerance, tacks), the optional measured refinement (mode A+: per-part sensor points labelled by CAD face, plane/quadric fits, fit-up diagnostic), mode B (no CAD: SAM2 per-part masks, PPF "no match", region growing + lit-quadric, reduced weldability rule, no library save), the selling point (graceful degradation), the order of work, and what validates what.
- **Status:** mode A done (2026-09-21) and validated on the robot (2026-09-24); tacks done (2026-09-21); mode A+ not built; mode B not built and "probably dropped" (TD/TP 2026-10-15, not decided); Phase 9 on hold; the stroke along the seam (4c) partly built (`stroke_mode:=seam`, first full-seam bench run still open per TD). The "4–10 entries ... A library part without an entry falls back to mode B" sentence is overtaken (it falls back to radius-PCA, RM §8).
- **Numbers held:** the benchmark's "seam transferred from the registered model" rung 0.95–1.00; lit-quadric pooled F1 0.87 given surfaces, 0.00 without surfaces/part membership; seam threshold 2.25 mm default vs the 1.1 mm reference gap; 2026-09-21 bench: ear 8–11 mm inside the base in one run, 1–8 mm above (tilted 1.6°, leaning 8°) in the other; registry 4 box parts verified at 0.000 mm, 10 meshes unsupported; 7 tests; 2026-09-24 contact depth −0.4/−0.6 mm, ~8 mm lateral residual.
- **Feeds:** 3.7 (why compute, not detect; pose tolerance), 3.8 (tacks), 3.7/7 (mode A+, mode B as future work), 5.4 (validation: fiducial bound, flatbed scan of pen marks), 6.4 (24 Sep row), Ch.4 link (benchmark rungs).
@@END
@S 3.7 | "So mode A does not detect; it computes. Detection is for the case where there is no CAD": the SEPC holds the CAD clouds at their ICP poses, so any detector run on them "only rediscovers, with error, what the poses already say"; in the WeldSet benchmark this regime is the "seam transferred from the registered model" rung, scoring 0.95–1.00 "because the model is the truth" — S2 §The finding
@S 3.7 | Mode A chain: RGB-D → SAM2 mask → PPF names the part → FoundationPose 6D → ICP-to-CAD → pose_static (A), pose_static (B) → weldgen primitives at those poses → D4 accessibility rule → every seam: analytic polyline, class, torch approach, weldable / reject reason; rejected candidates kept ("hard negatives the planner must avoid"); weld_generator an installable dependency (numpy 1.26 under Jazzy handled by the `_trapezoid` shim); "Its only error is the registration's" — carry ICP fitness / inlier_rmse with every seam — S2 Mode A 1-3
@S 3.7.2 | Pose tolerance origin (measured 2026-09-21 on two saved bench assemblies): the ear of `test_objv2` came back 8–11 mm inside the base in one run and 1–8 mm above it (tilted 1.6°, leaning 8°) in the other ("Single-view ICP does not observe a thin plate sliding in its own plane"); the generator's D4 rejects both (its gates assume contact) → `weld_pose_tol_mm` 10 mm "until the fiducial bound replaces it", never reject on fit-up, report `fitup_mm`; the seam line is invariant to the sliding error; third assembly (ear 2 mm in / 11 mm out end to end): the seam runs as far as the member stays within tolerance (`clip_registered`); a weldgen bug surfaced by real poses (`_mutually_visible` probing a degenerate segment lost both fillets of a T at any non-exact rotation) fixed in weld_generator with a regression test — S2 Mode A 3
@S 3.8 | Tacks done 2026-09-21: `compute_tacks` applies tackrule-0.1 unchanged to the mode-A seams; `tack_no` and `order` ("ends first, sides interleaved") "so the robot's reach ordering can start from either"; "The thesis' DP tack selection over a quality field is the later, richer replacement for the same slot" — S2 3b
@S 3.7 | Mode A+ (optional, not built): keep each part's segmented, background-subtracted sensor points at `save_object` in `static_frame`; label each point by the nearest face of the registered CAD; fit plane/quadric per face to the measured points (`lit_quadric.detect(points, region_labels=face, part_labels=part)`), intersect; benchmark: exact given the surfaces (pooled F1 0.87), immune to sensor noise, no far-side mirror in a single view; seam threshold must exceed the root gap (2.25 mm default vs the 1.1 mm reference gap); model-vs-measured seam = a fit-up diagnostic (real gap, warp) and a registration check — S2 Mode A 4
@S 3.7 | Mode B (no CAD, not built): SAM2 one mask per part (a click per part); PPF must return "no match" (server change; until then selected by hand); per-part sensor points in base_link; normal-based region growing; lit-quadric plane/quadric per surface, pairwise intersection across parts; reduced weldability rule (torch-cone clearance ray-cast against the measured cloud; D4's exact containment unavailable, stated); `source: sensor`; without surfaces and part membership the quadric method collapsed 0.87 → 0.00 on full-surface synthetic clouds (region growing leaks through a thin plate), so mode B "should sit between the two rungs" — a Phase 9 measurement; no library save in mode B (`save_object` refuses/warns without CAD) — S2 Mode B
@S 3.7 | Selling point: "A perception stack that degrades gracefully: with a CAD model the seam is exact by construction (registration error only); without one it falls back to the best geometric mechanism the benchmark found (quadric intersection) ... and the two are scored on the same real scans (Phase 9) against the same constructed truth" — S2 §The selling point (mode B now probably dropped)
@S 5.4 | Validation plan: "Mode A is validated by an independent pose measurement, not by the Phase 9 truth (which is itself derived from the same registration)": the fiducial board / jig bound on pose_static, and the pen-mark placement check on the UR5e (~0.1 mm from a flatbed scan) — "the placement ground truth the thesis already planned"; mode B and the seven benchmark methods validated on the Phase 9 scans — S2 §Validation
@S 6.4 | 2026-09-24: mode A validated on the robot: contact depth −0.4/−0.6 mm along the pen axis after the extrinsic re-solve and the pendant TCP; ~8 mm lateral residual "under attribution" — S2 Order of work 4b
@R 6.4 | 2026-09-24 first pen validation: contact depth −0.4 / −0.6 mm along the pen axis; ~8 mm lateral residual; single run — S2 4b, RM §14
@R 6.2 | 2026-09-21 two saved bench assemblies (single-view ICP): ear 8–11 mm inside the base / 1–8 mm above it, tilted 1.6°, leaning 8° — S2 Mode A 3
@O 3.7 | Mode A+ (per-part sensor points, face labelling, quadric refinement, fit-up diagnostic) — NOT BUILT — S2 order 2, TD
@O 3.7 | Mode B + PPF "no match" + refuse library save without CAD — NOT BUILT, probably dropped (TP decision 7) — S2 order 3
@O 3.9.4 | Draw the weldable seams with the pen (stroke along the whole seam; UR force mode or the admittance node along the pen axis) — `stroke_mode:=seam` exists; first full-seam bench run open (TD) — S2 order 4c
@O 5.4 | Flatbed scan of the pen marks (~0.1 mm) and a fiducial-board/jig bound on pose_static — planned, no result in any doc — S2 §Validation, RM §8
@O 3.8 | Plot q(s) before any selection code (the quality-field "week-two experiment") — not done — S2 order 5
@C L414-419 | seams computed, not detected — S2 ("mode A does not detect; it computes")
@C L440-441 | pose tolerance instead of contact tolerance — S2 Mode A 3
@M 3.7 | The one-sentence argument for computing seams (detecting on CAD clouds only rediscovers the poses; benchmark rung 0.95–1.00) and the graceful-degradation framing (mode A / A+ / B) belong in 3.7's opening or in Ch.4 §One Geometry; mode A+ and mode B in 7 future work — S2
@M 5.4 | ch5 L68-70 already names "ruler or flatbed scan": S2 gives the flatbed scan's planned resolution (~0.1 mm) and says the pen-mark check, not Phase 9 truth, validates mode A — 5.4 — S2 §Validation
@@DOC notes/curved_parts_print_list.md (PL, 2026-10-08, edited 2026-10-09)
- **Covers:** the parts to 3D print for the strata MDF cannot make: status (printing on a Creality K1 Max, 300 × 300 × 300 mm bed: C1, E2, R2, S3, RR1, SP3; BA parts laser-cut from MDF; "the cell is not building the dataset", one geometry per curved stratum is enough; MDF stock is 8 mm), what MDF covers, ranges vs weld_generator (`weldgen/d29.py`, phase9 §2), the full list of 22 geometries (C1–C3, E1–E3, R1–R2, S1–S4, RR1–RR3, SP1–SP3, BA1–BA3, G1–G3) with dimensions, drawing/printing notes (one watertight mm CAD, PLY + STL from the same model, datum, matte light filament, walls ≥ 3 mm, print orientation, brim, measure after printing, symmetry, fixtures), library clean-up candidates, and the per-part steps into the cell.
- **Status:** current for the six printed parts; the larger list (C2, C3, E1, E3, R1, S1, S2, S4, RR2, RR3, SP1, SP2, BA, G) is the dataset's Phase 9 wish list, not made for the cell; SP3 row updated 2026-10-09 (redrawn true offset). Note: "MDF stock is 8 mm: no plate thinner than 8 mm" conflicts with the 220 × 220 × 5.5 mm base plate introduced 2026-10-15 (BM, CS) — its material is not documented.
- **Feeds:** 5.3 (workpieces table and the TODO "dimensions per part; material"), 3.5.4 (symmetry), Ch.4 (strata), 7 (limitations: MDF/PLA instead of steel).
@@END
@S 5.3 | Printed parts and dimensions (mm): C1 T/circle pipe stub, square end, Ø 62 (r 31), wall 3, length 100; E2 T/ellipse tilted pipe, Ø 90, wall 5, tilt 25°; R2 T/saddle run pipe Ø 100 (R 50), wall 6, length 180; S3 T/saddle branch at 70° (tilt 20°) on R2, Ø 50 (r 25), wall 4, length 100; RR1 T/rounded_rect closed rounded-rectangle tube 64 × 64, corner R 12, wall 3, height 100; SP3 T/swept_path S-curve band (B-spline, 5 control points), span 220, band 5, height 80 (redrawn 2026-10-09 as a true 5 mm offset); printer Creality K1 Max (300 × 300 × 300 mm bed) — PL §Status, §The list
@S 5.3 | Material: plate strata (T/line, corner, butt square, lap, edge) and the base plates are MDF; MDF stock is 8 mm; BA (butt arc) parts laser-cut from MDF; curved strata and butt grooved 3D printed (butt grooved excluded for metal: it needs ≥ 6 mm plate the lab cannot process); filament matte and light (white/light grey): black, glossy or translucent filament gives the D435i holes or wrong depth — PL
@S 5.3 | Print rules: one CAD per part in mm, watertight; PLY (cell + server, same file name) and STL (print) from the same model ("A PLY that differs from the print places the pose wrong"); datum at a meaningful place (pipe axis at the base centre; a band's spine start on the bottom edge); walls ≥ 3 mm; bands and plates printed lying flat; pipes upright with the cut end up; a brim on the long run pipes; measure after printing (calipers) and record — PL §Drawing and printing notes
@S 5.3 | Fixtures: pipes and bands stand by themselves on the plate; the saddle runs (R1, R2) need two V-blocks (MDF possible) — PL §Drawing notes
@S 5.3 | Ranges: every size inside weld_generator's range (`weldgen/d29.py`, phase9 §2), so no "labelled extension" flag (the bought pipes' 2 mm walls were below the 3–8 mm range); Phase 9 rule per stratum: ≥ 3 distinct geometries and ≥ 2 wall/plate thicknesses — "The cell is not building the dataset" — PL §Ranges
@S 3.5.4 | Symmetry note: a plain pipe (C1–C3) can spin about its axis without the image changing; FoundationPose then needs `SYMMETRY_INFO` or its pose wanders about the axis; "The seam (a circle) does not care, but the tack positions along it do"; optional: a small flat or notch on the outer wall near the top (also in the CAD) breaks the symmetry; tilted, saddle and rounded-rect parts are not fully symmetric — PL §Drawing notes
@S 5.3 | Library clean-up candidates: `test_objv3.ply` (same outer size as `test_objv2.ply`, 256 × 250 × 108, hard to tell apart from one view); `plate.ply` vs `test_objv1_base.ply` (two thin rectangles 30 mm apart in one edge); `assembly_mesh.ply` in `CAD_DIR` (classified as a part); `Power Drill-ply.ply` (scanned, not a weld part, 20 MB); the composites `test_objv2.ply`, `test_objectv1.ply` — PL §The current library
@O 3.5.4 | Tack positions along a plain pipe's seam depend on its arbitrary spin (PL); `SYMMETRY_INFO` on the server not set (TD); optional notch not made — OPEN; state in 3.5.4/3.8 how tack phase is fixed on a symmetric loop (CS: "phase from the scene id") — PL, TD, CS
@O 5.3 | Record the measured (caliper) dimensions of each printed part — not reported in any doc (CS has only the CAD-fit values) — PL §Measure after printing
@O 5.3 | Material and thickness of the 220 × 220 × 5.5 mm base plate (PL says MDF stock is 8 mm only) — not documented — PL, BM
@X ch5 L51-57 | ch5 workpiece table: consistent with PL/CS for C1, E2, S3 on R2, RR1, SP3; add the dimensions (PL/CS), that the curved parts are PLA? prints (PL says "3D print" but never names the filament polymer — check) on MDF bases, the V-blocks for S3/R2, and the plate change; "two 8 mm MDF plates" is the nominal stock (RM §8 measured 8.4 mm) — PL, CS, RM §8
@M 5.3 | Printing and colour rules (matte light filament because the D435i fails on dark/glossy/translucent surfaces), walls ≥ 3 mm, same PLY/STL source — useful in 5.3 and 7 limitations — PL
@M 7 | Limitation: one geometry per curved stratum, printed parts and MDF instead of steel; the full Phase 9 list (≥ 3 geometries, ≥ 2 thicknesses per stratum) not made — 7 — PL
@@DOC notes/pen_marking_plan.md (PM, 2026-09-22..25)
- **Covers:** the pen-marking design after mode A: inputs/outputs; what existed (drawing stack, admittance node, move_to_object, RRT with joint limits only); the four phases per tack (transit, force-gated approach, mark, retract); options A (own capsule/box collision model vs MoveIt planning scene), B (force-gated descent vs position-only), C (dot vs stroke), D (branch lock, free roll, continuity, arch transfers), E (visiting order); decisions of 2026-09-22; milestones 1 (tool model), 2 (collision model), 3 (reachability, first bench run), 4 (marking node), 5 (first real touches and the hand-eye/TCP root causes), 6 (strokes); open questions (controller switching decided "none", F_touch 1.5 N, table); RViz frame diagnostics.
- **Status:** historical for the 2026-09-22..25 state, but the authoritative record of the design rationale and of milestone 5's calibration story. Superseded in parts: arch transfers → departure points + Cartesian line (CS 2026-10-14); roll per seam 15° steps → 30° grid and per-tack/DP on curved seams; "table a plane the pen never reaches, no fixtures" → table and fixtures in the model (CS 2026-10-14); "no controller switching" → the camera aim switches to the velocity controller (2026-10-15); "overshoot under 1 mm at 20 mm/s" estimate → the two-speed approach after ~10 N contacts (RM §14); pen tip lengths 0.19 m (assumed) → 183.78 mm (pendant, 09-24) → 181.7 mm (09-25) → `pen_tool.json` z 0.18091 m today.
- **Decisions (2026-09-22/24):** A1 own stack + capsule/box collision model (MoveIt planning scene as the upgrade path when real fixtures enter); B1 force-gated descent; C1 dot first, C2 stroke next; elbow-up by branch lock with the free roll; pen visits seam by seam in ascending `tack_no` (the rule's `order` is for the torch); F_touch = 1.5 N (pen not sprung; UR5e F/T noise ~0.5 N); no Servo, no controller switching for marking (2026-09-24).
- **Feeds:** 3.3.1–3.3.2 (TCP and hand-eye incidents), 3.8 (visit order vs weld order), 3.9.1–3.9.4, 3.10.2, 5.1 (tool), 5.4 (measurement), 6.1/6.4 (24 Sep numbers), 7 (MoveIt upgrade path, force mode).
@@END
@S 3.9 | No heat, so the literature's thermal arguments (work/travel angles, gravity, distortion sequencing) do not apply to the pen; "its motion hierarchy does" — PM intro
@S 3.9 | Per-tack motion (design): P_app = point + d_standoff·approach (d_standoff 35 mm); 1 transit (RRT in joint space, collision-checked); 2 approach LIN along −approach at 20 mm/s, force watched, stop at |F| > F_touch or at point + 3 mm overshoot → "no contact"; 3 mark; 4 retract LIN to P_app — PM §The motion
@S 3.9.2 | The pen axis is the tack's `approach` (the cleared bisector: 45° to both plates of a fillet, so the tip lands in the root); the pen is axisymmetric, so the roll about the pen axis is free — "that one degree of freedom is what we spend on reachability and on keeping the arm elbow-up, never a branch flip" — PM §The motion
@S 3.9.4 | "Contact is the measurement": the tip position at |F| > F_touch along the approach line vs the commanded tack point is the seam-root placement error along the approach axis; the lateral error comes from the mark itself (photo or caliper against a scribed reference line); both into `tack_marks.json` — PM §The motion
@S 3.9.1 | Option A1 chosen (2026-09-22): own collision model (UR5e links + pen as capsules, workpieces as the registered boxes, table as a half-space; capsule–box distance closed form, "the '8-cylinder' model of Liu et al. 2023"), our RRT-Connect, Cartesian LIN by IK, TOTG; ~150 lines + tests; risk: "our checker is only as good as its capsules; fixtures/clamps must be added by hand"; A2 (MoveIt 2 planning scene, FCL against CAD meshes, SRDF self-collision) is the upgrade path "the moment clamps and fixtures with real shapes enter the cell, or self-collision becomes a concern" — PM §Options A
@S 3.9.4 | Option B1 chosen: force-gated descent; "The registration error we measured is 4–11 mm (fit-up). Position-only marking would put the pen 5 mm into the plate or 5 mm above it. Force gating turns that error into a number instead of a crash, and the number is the validation" — PM §Options B
@S 3.9.4 | Option C: C1 dot first (touch, dwell, retract), C2 stroke (follow the seam for the tack length holding F_hold along the pen axis; shows the seam direction, makes the lateral error measurable along a line) next — PM §Options C
@S 3.8 | Visiting order for the pen: "There is no heat, so the rule's `order` (heat-balance) is not the right order for marking. Use travel ... seam by seam, `tack_no` ascending within a seam. Keep `order` in the file for the day the torch replaces the pen" — decided 2026-09-22; the code's `marking.visit_order` still says "Seam by seam, `tack_no` ascending - the pen's order (no heat to balance)" — PM §E, Decisions; marking.py L531-534
@S 3.9.2 | Branch lock (design): read the branch off the current elbow-up pose and reject IK solutions on another branch; all tack poses solved before moving; a tack needing a flip is retried with another roll, then reported unreachable; "Never flip mid-sequence"; as built (2026-09-24): scan home (UR order) (−0.34, −1.44, −1.56, −1.55, +1.94, −0.86), lock = signs of lift, elbow and wrist_2 = (−, −, +) (`marking.json` `branch_joints` [1, 2, 4]); IK (damped LS) seeded from the previous solution and the home pose — PM §D, M3; config/marking.json
@S 3.9.2 | Roll per seam (design 15° steps → built 30° grid, work angles 0/±10/±20°): per seam every (tilt, roll) tried on all tacks ascending; admissible = branch-locked approach pose + tack pose + LIN descent all clear, joint step ≤ 69° between tacks; winner = largest minimum clearance, then least travel; pose-only checks of the tool envelope before IK make the grid cheap — PM M3
@S 3.9.2 | First reachability run (2026-09-24, `test_objv2` T, ear leaning 8°): obtuse side (98°) reachable at tilt 0, roll 330°, 10.6 mm minimum clearance; acute side (82°) NOT at 5 mm: the holder body (radius 42 mm, 70 mm up the pen) reaches 70·sin(41°) − 42 = 3.9 mm from the plates at the bisector; "That is geometry, not planning": a longer pen or a slimmer holder (each +10 mm of reach adds ~6.6 mm in an 82° corner), or accept 3 mm; with the tip at 183.78 mm the holder sits 63.8 mm above it and clears a 90° T by 3.1 mm → default clearance 3 mm; with the tip at 181.7 mm: 61.7·sin 45° − 42 = 1.6 mm (tests at 1.5 mm, bench config 3 mm); "the marking node judges with the clearance recorded in tack_reach.json, never its own" — PM M3, M5, M6
@S 3.9.1 | Milestone 1 tool model (2026-09-22): `pen_tool.json` + `tool_model.py` + `tool_model_marker_node.py`: pen tip, touch force, standoff, a conservative envelope (flange adapter box, holder capsule, pen capsule, camera arm box) and the camera box placed BY THE CALIBRATION, "never typed"; verify in RViz that the boxes cover the real holder/camera ("they are envelopes - err large"); current config: flange_adapter box, holder_flange capsule (r 48 mm), holder_body capsule (r 20.5 mm), pen capsule (r 8 mm), camera_arm box, camera_body box — PM M1; config/pen_tool.json
@S 3.9.1 | Milestone 2 collision model (2026-09-22, `collision.py`, 7 tests): six arm capsules on the joint frames, upper-arm and forearm tubes displaced by the URDF's shoulder/elbow offsets (0.138 / 0.007 m); tool envelope by FK; registered parts as boxes; table as a plane; closed-form distances (segment–segment, golden-section segment–box, separating-axis box–box, lowest point vs plane); `is_valid` = joint limits AND clearance; `report(q)` prints the worst pairs; stated gaps: no full self-collision (the tool is checked against the base column, shoulder and upper arm; the branch lock does the rest), no fixtures (magnets), box–box is a yes/no test — PM M2
@S 3.9.3 | RRT-Connect fixes from milestone 3: the path was assembled in the wrong order after an odd number of tree swaps (fixed); edge check `edge_resolution` (default 0.05 rad; the pen planner uses 0.01 rad, ~5 mm of tip travel per sample, "so an 8 mm plate cannot slip between two samples") — PM M3
@S 3.9.4 | Milestone 4 marking node (2026-09-24): descent = a FollowJointTrajectory goal at 20 mm/s tip speed (2 mm IK steps) cancelled at |F − bias| > 1.5 N, the bias re-measured at each approach point over 0.5 s; the joints at the cancel = the contact record (`tack_marks.json`: tip at contact, depth along the pen axis, force, or `no_contact` after the 3 mm overshoot); gates: current joints clear and on the locked branch, first point within 20° of the current joints, |F| > 8 N at any time cancels; `dry_run:=true` pretends contact at the registered point; UR's `force_mode_controller` and `tool_contact_controller` loaded but inactive (the latter's threshold fixed and probably above 1.5 N) — PM M4
@S 3.9.4 | Controller switching DECIDED 2026-09-24: none — no Servo node in the running stack; the descent is a slow JointTrajectory on the existing `scaled_joint_trajectory_controller` with a wrench watchdog that cancels at |F| > 1.5 N; "At 20 mm/s with ~30–50 ms of cancel latency the overshoot is under 1 mm" (later contradicted on rigid parts: ~10 N at 10 mm/s, RM §14 → two-speed approach); force holding for strokes by short incremental trajectories (a 1-DoF admittance loop on the JTC at ~20 Hz); F_touch = 1.5 N because UR5e F/T noise is ~0.5 N; the low-pass must not delay the stop by more than ~1 mm (50 ms at 20 mm/s) — PM §Open questions
@S 3.9.4 | Milestone 6 stroke (2026-09-25): `stroke_mode` dot / tack (each tack's segment p0→p1) / seam (the whole weldable polyline, once per seam); contact-referenced: the stroke runs at (measured contact depth + `press_m`) along the pen; drawn in `stroke_chunk_m` (10 mm) chunks as successive goals; between chunks the depth moves by `depth_gain_m_per_n` × (hold force − mean force along the pen), clipped to `max_depth_step_m` ("a first-order admittance at chunk rate"); two chunks under `min_contact_force_n` = lifted off → stop; force sign read at the contact; per-chunk depth and mean force recorded; retract straight back along the pen — PM M6
@S 3.9.4 | Twin: Isaac exposes joint commands as a topic, not the trajectory action, so the twin run is the dry run with tip path and reach markers in RViz; first goals on the real robot one tack at a time (`~/next`) with the pendant's speed slider low — PM M4
@S 3.9.3 | Bench feedback (2026-09-24 late): the transit is re-planned from the arm's CURRENT joints at `~/next`/`~/home` (planning at `~/plan` and jogging in between made the first point 48–60° away; later replaced by joining the planned path, CS 2026-10-14); a rejected goal is reported with its usual cause (External Control program not running after the pendant was used — press Play) — PM M5
@S 3.3.1 | Pen TCP (2026-09-24 night): the pendant's TCP is the pen tip, (0, 0, 183.78) mm cap off (192.5 with the cap, not used); earlier `pen_tip_m` was ASSUMED (0, 0, 0.19) — "Z right + XY off is the signature of a pen tip that is not on the flange axis"; 4-point TCP least squares R_i d + t_i = p (`pen_tip_touchoff.py`); the nominal-FK probe read the tip at (2.9, −0.9, 182.5) vs the pendant's (0, 0, 183.78), ~3 mm at the tip; current `pen_tool.json` `pen_tip_m` = (0.00107, −0.00135, 0.18091) m — PM M1, M5; config/pen_tool.json
@S 3.3.2 | Milestone 5 root cause (2026-09-24): first touches: tack 0.1 no contact; 0.2 at 1.61 N +18.6 mm early; 1.1 at 1.72 N +28.9 mm early; 1.2 no contact — "a yaw plus a height error, i.e. an extrinsic ROTATION error"; offline in `handeye_samples.npz`: the 15 samples are fine (rotation diversity 110°) and a Park–Martin re-solve fits them to 2–4 mm std; the stored file did NOT fit its own samples (board-in-base spread 32/26 mm std, 120 mm range, 8° rotation): OpenCV's TSAI solve had failed silently in July; re-solved camera at (59, 78, 32) mm in tool0 with the optical axis along the flange normal vs (24, 91, 34), 7° tilt / 26° roll in the bad file — PM M5
@S 3.3.2 | The 6 cm located (2026-09-24 evening): re-solved optical origin (59, 78, 32) mm vs the camera body in RViz at (0, 90, 45): same radius from the flange axis (~98 vs 90 mm), 37° apart in azimuth = the camera seen from a TCP frame rotated about the tool axis; `tcp_offset_probe.py` measures D = T_tool0_tcp; the July capture ran under an unknown pendant TCP so it could not be repaired by composition → recapture with `extract_extrinsics.py --tcp-offset 0 0 0.18378 0 0 0` (composes and saves the camera in tool0, prints the residual judge); a good capture: 15–20 poses, board 30–50 cm from the camera filling a good part of the frame, rotations of ±30° or more about two axes, the arm at rest ~1 s before each capture — PM M5
@S 3.3.2 | OpenCV 5 pitfall (2026-09-24): the venv's `opencv-python 5.0.0` (shadowing `opencv-contrib-python 4.13`) dropped `interpolateCornersCharuco`, `estimatePoseCharucoBoard`, `calibrateHandEye`; `extract_extrinsics.py` now detects via `CharucoDetector.detectBoard` + `matchImagePoints` + `solvePnP` and solves hand-eye with numpy Park–Martin; `test_charuco_detection.py` renders a board at a known pose and recovers it to < 3 mm — PM M5
@S 3.3.2 | Attribution plan after the ~8 mm lateral error (2026-09-24): roll test (tool vs world), `pose_jitter_probe.py` (ICP noise floor), hand-eye recapture with wrist rolls (a rotation spread of 3.6° → ~9 mm at 0.5 m is the right size), calibrated FK, a pen probe measuring the root laterally — PM M5 result
@S 5.1 | Tool: pen in a holder, not spring-loaded; the D435i rides on the pen holder on the side opposite the connector; parts held by magnets (on holders above the table at the time) — PM Decisions 2026-09-22
@S 5.4 | Lateral error measurement (planned): photo or caliper of the mark against a scribed reference line; stroke vs the real seam by photo — PM §The motion, Milestones 6
@R 6.4 | First real touches (2026-09-24, old extrinsic): tack 0.2 +18.6 mm early at 1.61 N; tack 1.1 +28.9 mm early at 1.72 N; 0.1 and 1.2 no contact; single run — PM M5
@R 6.4 | After the recalibrated extrinsic + pendant TCP (2026-09-24 night): tack 1.2 contact at 1.54 N −0.4 mm, repeat 1.87 N −0.6 mm along the pen; tack 1.1 no contact within the 3 mm overshoot; ~8 mm lateral by eye; two touches of one tack — PM M5 result
@R 6.1 | Hand-eye (2026-09-24): 15 samples, rotation diversity 110°; Park–Martin fit 2–4 mm std; bad July file: board-in-base spread 32/26 mm std, 120 mm range, 8° rotation; camera (59, 78, 32) mm vs bad (24, 91, 34), 7° tilt / 26° roll; ChArUco synthetic recovery < 3 mm — PM M5
@R 6.8 | Reach (2026-09-24, T-joint, ear leaning 8°): obtuse side (98°) 10.6 mm at tilt 0 / roll 330°; acute side (82°) 3.9 mm (< 5 mm required) — PM M3
@O 3.9.1 | MoveIt 2 planning scene (FCL, SRDF self-collision) "when fixtures become real" — future/upgrade path — PM §Options A, TD §LATER
@O 3.9.4 | UR `force_mode_controller` for strokes (loaded, inactive) — OPEN (TD: holding 1.5 N on metal) — PM M4, TD

@X L563-570 | ch3 describes the descent profile and the 1.5 N stop but not WHY the bias is re-measured (F/T drift: 7 N at rest after a power cycle) nor the cancel mechanism (a FollowJointTrajectory goal at 20 mm/s cancelled by a wrench watchdog; joints at the cancel = the contact; ~30–50 ms cancel latency) — consistent, add — PM M4, §Open questions, TN Earlier
@X L499-501 | tool envelope: PM/config list a flange-adapter BOX, holder capsules (flange r 48 mm, body r 20.5 mm), the pen capsule (r 8 mm), a camera-arm BOX and the camera-body BOX placed by the hand-eye calibration — ch3's "capsules for the pen and holder, boxes for the camera body" omits the flange adapter and camera arm and that the camera box follows the calibration — PM M1, config/pen_tool.json
