@@DOC notes/aps_transit_plan.md (AP, 2026-10-06)
- **Covers:** why the transits needed more than the first RRT-Connect path (2026-10-06 bench: tack 0's transit 277 path points, 14.3 s; tack 2's 161); the status (steps 1–4 done, APS default; the first bench run that evening gave marks 2.3–2.7 mm); what was built; the equivalence and speed measurements of the C++ collision model; two porting bugs; what APS is (OMPL 1.7: parallel planners, hybridization, shortcutting, anytime) and why the Python wheels could not be used; the design D1–D5 (pybind11 module with the GIL released; a faithful C++ port of `collision.py` fed by `export_spec()`; the planning problem in scaled joint coordinates with weights (2, 2, 1.5, 1, 1, 0.5); hand-off to spline + TOTG; integration with fallbacks); steps 1–8.
- **Status:** current for the design and measurements; parameter values partly superseded (`transit_budget_s` 1.0 here → 3.0 s since 2026-10-14, code; the "unwrap the goal to the 2π-equivalent nearest the start" endpoint rule was replaced by the ±180° wrist_3 window on 2026-10-16, TN). Step 5 (benchmark on a real session) and step 8 (box–box clearance) still open; the multi-view planner still uses RRT-Connect.
- **Feeds:** 3.9.1 (C++ model, distance algorithms, quirks), 3.9.3 (APS, scaled coordinates, weights, fallbacks, hand-off), 3.10.2, 6.6, 6.7, App. A.
@@END
@S 3.9.3 | Problem (2026-10-05/06): the marking node's transits came from the first RRT-Connect path; shortcut, spline and TOTG smoothed its corners "but its route still deviates too much"; 2026-10-06 bench: tack 0's transit had 277 path points and took 14.3 s; tack 2's had 161 — AP intro
@S 3.9.3 | APS (OMPL 1.7 `AnytimePathShortening`): (1) runs several planners (RRT-Connect by default) in parallel threads; (2) keeps their solution paths; (3) hybridizes them (`PathHybridization`: splices the best segments of different paths where they cross); (4) shortcuts the result (`PathSimplifier`); (5) repeats until the time runs out; "The result can beat every single seed, and it improves with time" — AP §What APS is
@S 3.9.3 | Why C++: neither Python wheel has the class (checked 2026-10-06: `ompl` 2.0.1 exposes no APS and no PathHybridization; `ompl` 1.7.0 exposes PathHybridization but not APS); a Python collision callback would make the threads queue on the GIL → the official C++ APS from ROS's own `libompl` 1.7 (`ros-jazzy-ompl`), nothing pip-installed — AP §What APS is
@S 3.9.3 | D3 planning problem: 6-D `RealVectorStateSpace` in scaled coordinates y_j = w_j·q_j (bounds w·joint limits) so path length IS the weighted joint travel (`PathLengthOptimizationObjective`); weights (2, 2, 1.5, 1, 1, 0.5): "base and shoulder sweep the most space"; validity = C++ `StateValidityChecker` wrapping `is_valid` (q = y/w) with `DiscreteMotionValidator` at no joint moving more than `edge_resolution` 0.01 rad between checks; `num_planners` RRT-Connect (default 4, one per thread), each with the RRT's `range` 0.15 rad (scaled); `setHybridize(true)`, `setShortcut(true)`, `setMaxHybridizationPath(max_paths)`; `timedPlannerTerminationCondition(budget_s)`; stats (solve time, number of solutions and hybridizations, first and final cost, validity calls); `ompl::RNG::setSeed` per call (threads not fully deterministic: tests check properties) — AP D3
@S 3.9.3 | D4/D5 hand-off and fallbacks: the APS path goes to `marking.smooth_transit` (the spline, every sample re-checked by the PYTHON model: "a second, independent check of the C++ result"), then TOTG; `shortcut_path` skipped; `transit_path` checks the straight edge first; if `_transit_cpp` is not built or APS finds no path, fall back to RRT-Connect and log why; all transits planned at `~/plan`, cost ≈ budget × transits (~5 s for 4 tacks + home at the then 1 s budget) — AP D4, D5
@S 3.9.3 | Original endpoint rule (2026-10-06): "Python unwraps the goal to the 2π-equivalent nearest the start (`_unwrap_to`; `unwrap=False` for home)" — the continuity that later wound the camera cable (TN 2026-10-16) and was replaced by the ±180° wrist_3 window — AP D3, TN
@S 3.9.1 | D2 C++ collision model = faithful port fed by `CollisionModel.export_spec()` ("the two cannot drift apart"): kinematics (6 joint origins, nominal or the factory calibration), frame chain (Rz(π) base, joint origin·Rz(q_i), tool0 = …·rpy(0, −π/2, −π/2)·rpy(π/2, 0, π/2)), 6 arm capsules (base, shoulder, upper_arm, forearm, wrist_1, wrist_2) with `SHOULDER_OFFSET` 0.138 and `ELBOW_OFFSET` 0.007 and radii, tool primitives (capsules, boxes), scene boxes (centre, R, half), table (`table_z`, `table_exempt`: base, shoulder), tool-vs-arm pairs (`self_pairs`: base, shoulder, upper_arm), clearance and joint limits — AP D2
@S 3.9.1 | Distance algorithms (same in both languages): segment–segment closed form; point–box in the box frame; segment–box golden-section search to `tol` 1e-4 plus both ends; box–box separating-axis test (0 or +∞); table = the lowest point of the primitive; `is_valid(q)` = within joint limits AND min distance ≥ clearance, const and thread-safe; known quirk kept on purpose: a tool BOX (the camera body) gets no clearance against a part box, only "not touching" — AP D2
@S 3.9.1 | Porting findings: `py::array_t<bool>(n)` filled through `mutable_unchecked` returned every element as the last row's verdict → `is_valid_many` builds the array from explicit bytes; the Python `_edge_valid` resolution is in a weighted metric (wrists weighted 0.3–0.5) so it lets a wrist move up to ~0.03 rad between checks, while the C++ side checks ≤ 0.01 rad on every joint ("at least as strict") — AP §Two findings
@S 3.10.2 | D1: `admittance_control/_transit_cpp` is a pybind11 module of the package (not a ROS service: synchronous, nothing serialized, unit-testable without ROS); `pybind11-dev` 2.11; built by CMake (`find_package(ompl)`, `find_package(pybind11 CONFIG)`, `pybind11_add_module`); the GIL released while planning — AP D1
@S 6.6 | Decision criterion for APS (AP step 6): "a clearly shorter route at a 1 s budget; the whole `~/plan` within a few seconds" — then `transit_planner` = `aps` by default (done 2026-10-06) — AP steps
@S App.A | Planner parameters as built 2026-10-06: `transit_planner` aps, `transit_budget_s` 1.0 (3.0 since 2026-10-14), `aps_num_planners` 4, `aps_max_paths` 8, `edge_resolution` 0.01 rad, RRT `range` 0.15 rad, weights (2, 2, 1.5, 1, 1, 0.5); code today also `transit_max_budget_s` 10.0 and `aps_clearance_margin_m` 0.002 ("APS plans this much wider than the Python re-check") — AP, tack_marking_node.py L112-118
@R 6.6 | `test/test_transit_cpp.py` (2026-10-06): FK equal to the Python frames to 1e-12 (nominal and calibrated); `is_valid` identical on 2 × 4 400 configurations (random + near contact) in the T-joint scene and a real session with the table; distances equal to 1e-9 (one exact tie resolved differently in the last bit, accepted); speed 8.6 µs per check vs 5 100 µs in Python (~590×); APS front↔back T-joint transit at 1 s: weighted joint travel 2.9 vs 17.6–18.0 for a single RRT-Connect (median of 5), ~6× shorter; APS threads CPU/wall 4.8; full plan at 0.5 s budget: straight moves stay straight, the front↔back transit and home through APS in ~0.5 s each — AP §Measured (the 6× is a median of 5 RRT-Connect runs vs APS; tool-tip length not measured here)
@R 6.4 | 2026-10-06 evening, first bench run with APS: marks 2.3–2.7 mm off, planning fast; roll fallback and 2 mm APS margin added — AP §Status
@O 6.6 | AP step 5: `scripts/benchmark_transits.py` on a real session (every transit + home; RRT-Connect + shortcut + spline vs APS at 0.5/1/2 s and 2/4 planners; planning time, weighted joint travel, tool-tip path length, max deviation from the straight line, smallest clearance, TOTG duration; table + JSON → TN) — OPEN — AP steps, TD
@O 3.9.1 | AP step 8: box–box clearance for the camera body (FCL or exact box–box distance) in both models with the equivalence test kept green — OPEN — AP, TD
@O 3.6.1 | Multi-view view-to-view transits through APS — OPEN — AP §Still to do, TD
@C L544-551 | APS = parallel RRT-Connect + hybridization + shortcutting, time budget; scaled joint coordinates y_j = w_j q_j; W = diag(2, 2, 1.5, 1, 1, 0.5) — AP D3 (exact)
@C L553-554 | smoothed (spline), re-checked (by the Python model, independently of the C++ planner), TOTG — AP D4
@C L597-598 | C++ collision model + planner (pybind11), "one plan from minutes to seconds" — AP, TN, CS
@X L503-504 | (supporting) capsule–box distance is a golden-section search "to `tol` 1e-4, plus both ends" (numerical, convex in the segment parameter), not a closed form; box–box SAT returns 0 or +∞ — AP D2
@X ch6 L92-94 | ch6 "front-back transit ~6× shorter at a 1 s budget": AP — weighted joint travel 2.9 vs 17.6–18.0 (median of 5 RRT-Connect runs) on the T-joint; and "identical on 8 800 configurations" = 2 × 4 400 (random + near-contact) in two scenes; "~590×" = 8.6 µs vs 5 100 µs per check — add the measures and the scenes — AP §Measured
@M 3.9.3 | Why APS had to be C++ (no APS in the Python wheels; GIL would serialize threads) and the equivalence-testing method (near-contact configurations, the pybind11 bool-array bug, the last-bit tie) — 3.9.3/3.10.2 — AP, TN 2026-10-06 (evening)
@M 3.9.3 | Hybridization explained (splicing the best segments of different paths where they cross) and that APS "can beat every single seed" — 3.9.3 / 2.7 — AP
@@DOC notes/perception_aciklama.md (PA, Turkish; 2026-10-04..06)
- **Covers:** a step-by-step explanation (written for the advisor after the 2026-10-01 meeting) of one RGB-D frame's trip laptop → desktop server → laptop, with the shape, unit and frame of the data at every step: overview diagram; the D435i (streams, K, frame, alignment); the bridge (sync ≤ 0.25 s, PNG, camera.json, multipart POST, 300 s timeout); the CAD files (locations, PLY format in mm, the library table with sizes); the server's input handling; SAM2 (prompted segmentation, Hiera encoder, 3 candidate masks for a single click, no ground removal, click priorities); the masked-depth → point-cloud steps (erode 2 px, dilate 4 px for normals, 12-NN normals, voxel, ≤ 2000 points); PPF in depth (features, model clouds, `trainModel` hash tables, `match` voting, 12 hypotheses, ICP, coverage × explained, size pre-filter, why the PPF pose is discarded, limits); FoundationPose (inputs/outputs, why it, 252 hypotheses, 5 refiner passes, scorer, notes); the reply JSON and artifacts; the laptop side (checks, topics); live FP tracking on the laptop (who does what, one frame step by step, ego-motion, fit, states, measurements on the RTX 4060, limits, how to run); a data summary table; timings; things to check and the 2026-10-01 meeting's open questions.
- **Status:** current for perception (§12 added 2026-10-06). Partly overtaken: §12.4's "the bridge stamps detections with the publish time" was fixed (TN 2026-10-06: registered frame's stamp); "ICP node handing tracking to `/perception/fp/pose` is the next step" was done 2026-10-06 (RF step 6); §15's depth-alignment worry is answered by RM L508/PMAP (`realsense_camera_node` publishes depth aligned to colour) but the checkbox is not ticked; "tracking accuracy not measured" still true.
- **Numbers held:** K fx 919.47, fy 918.94, cx 650.85, cy 350.62; 1280×720 RGB and depth; depth valid 1 mm–3 m (ZFAR); model clouds 60 000 → ~600 points; OpenCV grid 25 cells/axis → 339–591 points; pairs 115 000–349 000 per model; distance bins 4 % of the diameter, angle bins 12°; scene ~490 points, ~98 reference points, ~48 000 pairs per model; 55 clusters → 12 hypotheses; ICP 50 iterations; τ = 8 mm; size pre-filter +15 % / 45 % (8 of 13 models eliminated); FP 42 × 6 = 252 hypotheses, 5 refiner passes; FP score 67.97; PPF score 0.588, margin 0.438; classification ~1.4 s (13 models), match ~0.07 s per model; tracking 28.3/43.2 ms (1/2 passes), jitter 0.5 mm / 0.16–0.19° on a recorded frame; ~250 MB GPU; 25–29 Hz, 70–90 ms; LOST in 3 frames on a 1.5 s blackout (fit 0.90 on recovery); re-registration 3.9–4.0 s; ~8 Hz during a same-GPU registration; register() > 8 GB (scorer 2.6 GB at once), `REGISTER_CROP=1` ≤ 4.3 GB (unverified); old SAM-6D chain 1–1.5 min.
- **Feeds:** 3.5.1, 3.5.2, 3.10.1, 5.1, 5.2, 6.2, 6.5, 6.7, 2.4 (PPF/FoundationPose/SAM2 explanations), App. A.
@@END
@S 3.5.1 | The two machines share one key: the CAD file name; both CAD folders must hold the same files with the same names AND contents ("the server computes the pose for its file; if the laptop's file has another origin or axis the same pose lands elsewhere"); a new part must be added on both sides (`/add_model`) — PA §1, §4.1
@S 3.5.1 | CAD format: binary little-endian PLY triangle meshes, vertices x, y, z float32, no normals/colour/texture; unit mm, the server scales by `MESH_SCALE` = 0.001 (FreeCAD export units are a per-document setting: check) — PA §4.2
@S 3.5.1 | Bridge capture: RGB and depth paired only if their stamps differ ≤ 0.25 s; RGB → rgb.png (8-bit), depth → depth.png (16-bit, mm), camera.json {"cam_K": [9], "depth_scale": 1.0}; one multipart POST to http://<desktop>:5000/predict_pose; timeout 300 s (the server may wait for a click) — PA §3
@S 3.5.1 | Server input: three files saved to `Data/Input/`; RGB read BGR → RGB; depth to metres Z = raw × depth_scale / 1000; invalid depth set to 0 (< 1 mm or ≥ 3 m, `ZFAR`); size mismatch → rejected; one request at a time (a second gets HTTP 503 "busy": one GPU, one click window) — PA §5
@S 3.5.1 | SAM2 (`sam2.1_hiera_small`): input only the RGB image and one or more clicks (depth not used); output a 720×1280 binary mask; image encoder (Hiera) once per image (the expensive part), prompt encoder (position + label 1/0), mask decoder (+ predicted IoU); a single click is ambiguous ("the whole part", "this face" or "this hole") → 3 candidate masks requested, the highest predicted score taken; several clicks → one mask; no separate ground removal: the click is on the part, the table is another region, overflow carved with a negative click — PA §6
@S 3.5.1 | Click sources by priority: a client `mask` file (SAM2 skipped) > a client `click` field (no display needed) > a window on the server (left click object, right click not-object, ENTER) — PA §6.3
@S 3.5.1 | Masked depth → scene cloud (`scene_cloud_from_mask()`, `ppf_classifier.py`): mask eroded 2 px (edge pixels mix part and table depth, a "skirt"); a 4 px dilated version back-projected only for normals; normals from a plane fit to the 12 nearest neighbours (OpenCV `computeNormalsPC3d`), flipped towards the camera (CAD normals point out, so scene normals must face the camera or the PPF angles fail); the dilated rim then dropped; voxel cube edge = max(object diameter / 60, 1.5 mm); random subsample to ≤ 2000 points → N×6 (position + unit normal), metres, camera frame, partial (visible faces only) — PA §7.2-7.3
@S 3.5.1 | PPF basics: F = (‖d‖, ∠(n1,d), ∠(n2,d), ∠(n1,n2)) for an ordered point pair, invariant to rigid motion; ‖d‖ in metres so PPF is not scale-invariant, but depth is metric so there is no scale ambiguity; Drost et al., "Model Globally, Match Locally" (CVPR 2010) via OpenCV `cv2.ppf_match_3d`; verification score after Birdal & Ilic (3DV 2015); PPF is used as a CLASSIFIER: its output is a name and the score table, no pose — PA §8, §8.1
@S 3.5.1 | Model clouds (offline, `build_ppf_library.py`): mesh mm → m; 60 000 area-proportional random surface points with the exact triangle normal; voxel to ~600 points keeping a representative (not the mean: averaging rounds corners and makes normals belonging to no face); ~600×6 in the CAD frame saved in `Data/ppf_library.npz` — PA §8.2
@S 3.5.1 | `trainModel`: OpenCV's own sampling (bounding box in 25 cells per axis, step 0.04) leaves 339–591 points per model (`test_objv2_ear` 600 → ~543); all ordered pairs M(M−1) (≈ 294 000 for the ear; 115 000–349 000 per model); an α_m angle per pair; quantisation: distance bins of 4 % of the diameter, angles in 12° bins (360°/30); hash table key → [(m1, m2, α_m)]; one detector (table) per CAD; OpenCV cannot save the table, so the server retrains at start (~1–2 s per model) — PA §8.3-8.4
@S 3.5.1 | `match` (systematic, no randomness): the 2000-point scene downsampled on the same 25³ grid to ~490 points; every 5th point a reference point s_r (~98); each paired with all other scene points (~48 000 pairs per model); each pair's F looked up; each model pair proposes "s_r is m_r, rotate α = α_m − α_s about the normal" → 1 vote in s_r's [m_r, α] accumulator (model points × 30 angle bins); the top cell gives one full pose per reference point (generalised Hough) — PA §8.5
@S 3.5.1 | Verification: ~98 raw poses clustered by OpenCV (55 clusters for the ear, votes 884, 583, 459, 334, …); the code takes the top 12 (`n_hypotheses` = 12) because on symmetric or flat parts the most-voted pose can be a wrong orientation; votes are not comparable across models (a big self-similar model collects more), so per model: the 12 hypotheses refined by ICP (50 iterations; closes the 12° binning error), points not visible from the camera removed (back faces, self-occlusion), two-way score at τ = 8 mm: coverage = scene points within τ of the model / all scene points ("how much of the scene this model explains"), explained = visible model points within τ of the scene / all visible model points ("how much of what should be visible is there"), score = coverage × explained (0–1; coverage alone lets a big model win, explained alone a small one hiding inside); a model's score = its best hypothesis; highest wins; margin = lead over the runner-up — PA §8.6
@S 3.5.1 | Size pre-filter: scene diameter (99.5th percentile of pairwise distances) vs model diameter: drop the model if the scene is > 15 % bigger (a partial view cannot be bigger) or < 45 % of it (loose, for occlusion); on a real test frame 8 of 13 models were dropped; if all are dropped the filter is ignored — PA §8.7
@S 3.5.1 | Why the PPF pose is not used: it exists only to score whether a CAD explains the cloud; FoundationPose computes the pose from scratch; "FoundationPose's being more accurate was ASSUMED, not measured" (§15) — PA §8.8
@S 3.5.1 | PPF limits: a distinguishing feature out of view (e.g. a boss underneath) cannot be resolved from one view — a small margin "is not an error but a signal that the information is missing"; `plate` vs `test_objv1_base` very hard; thin curved parts (`270circle`) need a finer sampling step — PA §8.9
@S 3.5.2 | FoundationPose (NVIDIA, CVPR 2024; `estimater.py`, `EST.register()`): no retraining for new objects (networks trained on a large synthetic set to correct a pose from a render-vs-image comparison); input K, RGB 720×1280×3, depth (m), mask, the PPF-chosen PLY (in m); output T (4×4), the object in the camera frame; steps: depth erosion + bilateral filter; translation from the mask's bounding-box centre back-projected at the median mask depth; rotation hypotheses = 42 icosphere viewpoints × 6 in-plane rotations (60° apart) = 252, merged when closer than 30° or equal by symmetry; refiner network 5 passes per hypothesis (render colour + depth at the pose, crop the real RGB-D, predict the rotation/translation correction, re-render); scorer network ranks all refined hypotheses (also against each other); best returned — PA §9, §9.2
@S 3.5.2 | FP notes: the CADs have no colour/texture, so the render is flat grey and the comparison relies mostly on geometry and depth; the returned score (67.97 in the last run) is an unnormalised ranking score, not a probability; networks are object-independent and loaded once, switching parts is `reset_object()` (milliseconds), which is what lets PPF pick a different CAD per request; symmetric parts need `SYMMETRY_INFO` or the result may jump between equivalent poses — PA §9.2 notes
@S 3.5.2 | Reply: JSON {status, units "m", object_name, object_file, pose 4×4 (m, camera frame), score, elapsed_sec, classification {score, margin, runner_up, scores, rejected (e.g. "Tblock": "scene 255mm exceeds CAD 181mm by 41%"), elapsed_sec}, mask_source, artifacts (base64)}; artifacts detection_pem.json (R, t in MILLIMETRES, obj_name — the old SAM-6D format kept for the ICP node), detection_ism.npz (segmentation (1, 720, 1280)), object_name.txt, mask.png, vis_pose.png; the bridge rejects a reply whose units are not "m" ("mixing mm and m makes the pose 1000× wrong"), logs the top three PPF scores and the margin, warns if ambiguous, `min_score` default 0 (off), publishes latched (TRANSIENT_LOCAL) `/perception/detections` (frame `camera_color_optical_frame`, id/class_id = part name, quaternion) and `/perception/object_name` — PA §10-11
@S 3.5.2 | Live tracking architecture (added 2026-10-06): registration stays on the desktop (RTX 5070 Ti); tracking on the laptop's own RTX 4060 next to the camera; only one frame per registration crosses the network; the laptop container (`fp-sam2:ada`) runs `fp_track_server.py` (refiner only, `track_one` each frame, 2 refiner passes, no 252 hypotheses, no scorer: tens of ms); a WebSocket on the same laptop because the container is Ubuntu 22.04 / Python 3.10 without ROS while the workspace is ROS 2 Jazzy / Python 3.12 and Humble↔Jazzy DDS is not supported ("a few ms of copying") — PA §12, §12.1
@S 3.5.2 | Per frame: the node takes the newest RGB-D pair (≤ 2 waiting; older ones skipped, not queued); one binary message ~4.6 MB at 1280×720 with header (seq, stamp, K, depth scale, T_base_cam at the image time); the tracker converts depth to metres, applies the ego-motion prediction pose_pred = inv(T_base_cam_now)·T_base_cam_prev·pose_prev (skipped without TF, then arm motion looks like part motion), runs `track_one` (2 passes), computes fit = CAD pixels whose measured depth is within 10 mm of the render / rendered CAD pixels; states IDLE → (start) → TRACKING → (fit < 0.5 for 3 frames) → LOST → (fit ≥ 0.6) → TRACKING; while LOST no pose is published, the tracker retries cheaply from the last good pose each frame (a hand covering it briefly), and every 3 s the node asks the desktop for a click-free re-registration with the dilated CAD silhouette at the last good pose as mask — PA §12.2
@S 3.5.2 | Limits (2026-10-06): register() does not fit the 8 GB laptop (the scorer expands all 252 hypotheses to full-frame size at once, 2.6 GB in one go); `REGISTER_CROP=1` crops to the networks' region and fits (≤ 4.3 GB) but was NOT verified to give the same pose, so it is off; the fit check is fooled by flat parts on flat surfaces (a thin plate on the table fits the depth anywhere in-plane; a wrong pose scored 0.94 on the test frame); the test frame had no real part (a strip on a flat plate), so speed, latency and the LOST logic were measured but "the tracking's pose accuracy was not measured"; `use_roi` gains little locally — PA §12.4
@S 5.1 | D435i: stereo depth (two IR cameras + a projector) plus a separate RGB camera; IMU unused; RGB 8-bit 3-channel 1280×720; depth 16-bit Z (distance along the optical axis), raw unit 1 mm, 1280×720; colour K = [919.47 0 650.85; 0 918.94 350.62; 0 0 1] (1 mm at 1 m ≈ 0.92 px); `camera_color_optical_frame`: x right, y down, z forward; depth must be aligned to colour (driver topic `/camera/aligned_depth_to_color/image_raw`); minimum range ~28 cm — PA §2, §15
@S 5.2 | Laptop FP image: `docker build -f docker/Dockerfile.blackwell --build-arg TORCH_CUDA_ARCH_LIST=8.9 -t fp-sam2:ada .`, `docker/run_container.sh`, `python scripts/fp_track_server.py` (127.0.0.1:5001); ROS side `fp_tracker_node.py -p register_url:=http://<desktop>:5000/predict_pose`; services `~/start`, `~/stop`, `~/reseed` — PA §12.5
@S 5.3 | Library CADs (axis-aligned sizes, mm): `test_objv2_ear` 250 × 8 × 99 (8 vertices, the standing plate of the T); `test_objv2_base` 249 × 8 × 256 (the base, old); `test_objv2` 256 × 250 × 108 (base + ear composite); `test_objv3` same outer size; `test_objv1_base` 180 × 4 × 100; `test_objv1_ear` 54 × 4 × 100; `test_objectv1` 180 × 100 × 54; `plate` 150 × 4 × 100; `smallplate` 100 × 50 × 4; `vplate` 100 × 104 × 100 (V); `Tblock` 150 × 80 × 100; `270circle` 150 × 50 × 150 (270° thin curved ring, 384 vertices); `powerdrill` (Artec 3-D scan, 249 998 vertices, 20 MB); `assembly_mesh` 298 × 304 × 119 (written by the program) — PA §4.3
@S 6.7 | Timing table (2026-10-06): server start = FP networks + PPF training (~1–2 s per model) + SAM2, once; click: operator (none if sent); cloud + PPF classification ~1.4 s for 13 models (measured), of which `match` ~0.07 s per model (CPU, test frame); 12 hypotheses × ICP + score dominate (not measured separately); FP `register()` reported as `elapsed_sec` (not measured separately); whole chain observed < 10 s, not measured step by step; the old SAM-6D chain 1–1.5 min; registration answers in 1–4 s; tracking 28 ms (1 pass) / 43 ms (2 passes) per frame; image → pose 70–90 ms at 25–29 Hz; re-registration after LOST 3.9–4.0 s on the 4060 (not measured on the desktop) — PA §12, §14
@R 6.2 | PPF classification example (test frame): `test_objv2_ear` score 0.588, margin 0.438 over `test_objv2_base` (0.150); Tblock rejected by the size filter ("scene 255mm exceeds CAD 181mm by 41%"); classification 1.4 s; FP score 67.97; translation ≈ (164, 45, 499) mm; single frame — PA §9.2, §10.1
@R 6.5 | `track_bench.py` on the RTX 4060 (GPU only, a recorded 1280×720 frame, part still, 200 frames per row): 1 pass full frame 28.3 ms (p95 40.1) 35 Hz, jitter 0.5 mm / 0.16°; 2 passes full frame 43.2 ms (59.9) 23 Hz, 0.5 mm / 0.19°; 1 pass ROI 524×478 23.6 ms (34.8) 42 Hz, 0.7 mm / 0.22°; 2 passes ROI 38.8 ms (56.1) 26 Hz, 0.7 mm / 0.27°; tracking server ~250 MB GPU memory; end to end (test node publishing a recorded frame at 30 Hz, both servers on the same 4060): 25–29 Hz (target ≥ 15, goal 30), 70–90 ms raw / ~100 ms JPEG/PNG (target ≤ 100, goal 50); a 1.5 s blackout → LOST in 3 frames, recovered by itself (fit 0.90); re-registration 3.9–4.0 s; tracking fell to ~8 Hz during a registration on the same GPU; repeated frames of ONE static recorded image (jitter is a noise floor, not accuracy) — PA §12.3
